<summary>This is the title page of an alchemical work published in 1706, claiming to reveal the secret of the "Universal Solvent" and the "Chaotic Matter" required for the Great Work. The author uses a pseudonym, Alitophilus Chrysander, to remain anonymous while offering the text as a spiritual and scientific service.</summary>

<meta>This page serves as the formal preface or dedication of the work. The catchword "und" (and) at the bottom indicates that the sentence continues onto page 4, likely transitioning into the author’s intent to publish or share his findings.</meta>

# In the Name of the Triune GOD!

No one will be able to deny that there has perhaps never been a time when the learned and wise world has strived more in all kinds of sciences and arts to bring the truth—which until now has been rather hidden and obscured—back into the bright light of the sun, than in these present times, when it is beginning to break forth quite freely through both teachings and writings. This occurs especially in the <term>study of chemistry</term> <note>original: "Studio Chymico"; at this time, "chemistry" or "chymistry" often referred to a blend of alchemy, metallurgy, and pharmaceutical science</note>. In this regard, I have been moved above all by the writings of Licentiate Helwig <note>referring to Christoph Helwig (1581–1617), a noted physician and scholar</note>, a famous <term>chemist</term> and <term>physician</term> <note>original: "Chymici und Medici"</note> in Tennstedt and my highly trusted friend; his works certainly show the true path to the lovers of truth. Therefore, I have been moved to take that which God has so wonderfully bestowed upon me in the form of priceless <term>secrets</term> <note>original: "Secretis"; often referring to proprietary alchemical formulas or medicinal recipes</note> and share them with honest <term>chemists</term>...

<summary>In this introductory text, the author reflects on the "enlightened" nature of his era, noting a surge in scientific and artistic discovery. He specifically highlights the field of chemistry/alchemy and credits the writings of his friend, the physician Christoph Helwig, as the inspiration for sharing his own divinely granted "secrets."</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, chemistry, enlightenment, Christoph Helwig, Tennstedt, scientific progress, medical secrets, Early Modern science</keywords>

For since I have, unfortunately, been placed by my God into such a difficult office and many duties that it is impossible for me—much as I would like—to investigate these highly important <term>secrets</term> <note>original: "Secreta"; specifically referring to alchemical formulas or hidden natural laws</note>, and yet it could easily happen that these same things might be torn up or otherwise destroyed at or after my death, or through other accidents, I have preferred to bring such <term>secrets</term> into the bright light of the sun for the honor of God and for the pleasure and benefit of all <term>chemical</term> enthusiasts, rather than having them hidden away in some unknown corner. Brief Report /

A special secret <note>original: "Secret"; here referring to a private or hidden laboratory recipe</note> that I obtained and kept from the manuscripts of a good friend who was most deeply acquainted with these matters. He eventually attained the so-called <term>$\ominus$</term> <gloss>Salt</gloss> of Nature and performed incomparable cures with it; I received this after his death.

### A Brief Report on the Great Tincture, or the Philosopher’s Stone.

This Work can be made from many things; and indeed, it is certain that it can be prepared from all three kingdoms <note>original: "ex omnibus tribus regnis"</note>, namely the Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral, as well as from others; however, it must be noted that one path is more difficult than another. Some have derived it from mountain clay or potter's clay; some from human blood <note>original: "ex Sanguine humano"</note>, as Aristotle says in his treatise to Alexander the Great in the *Alchemical Seven-Stars* <note>A reference to a popular alchemical compendium; the "Aristotle" mentioned here is likely a pseudo-Aristotelian author common in medieval alchemy</note>, page 69. Some [derive it] from mercury ore; some, like Bernard of Trevis <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist</note>, from mercury itself. Very many [derive it] from vitriol <term>Vitriol: A sulfate mineral, usually of iron or copper, often used as a primary acidic solvent in the laboratory.</term>; others from urine and wine <note>original: "ex Uro und Vino"</note>; some, like Philaletha <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, the pseudonym of a prominent 17th-century alchemist</note>, from mercury and <term>Stibium</term> <gloss>Antimony</gloss>, some from <term>$\odot$</term> <gloss>Gold (the Sun)</gloss> and <term>Stibium</term> <gloss>Antimony</gloss>; or from <term>Stibium</term> <gloss>Antimony</gloss> and <term>☽</term> <gloss>Silver (the Moon)</gloss>, as the Englishman John Dastin <note>A 14th-century alchemical author</note> shows in his *Rosarium*, where he says at the end of Chapter III: The entire work of this art consists, therefore, in <term>Stibium</term> <gloss>Antimony</gloss> and <term>Gold</term>, in <term>Stibium</term> and <term>Silver</term>; but it is necessary that you labor for their dissolution, and bring them into their

### Concerning the Great Tincture.

Note: The first Matter. See the *Alchemical Seven-Stars* <note>original: "Alchymistisches Sieben-Gestirn"; a well-known 17th-century alchemical compendium</note> page 115. I myself have a manuscript of an Adept <term>Adept: An alchemist who has successfully discovered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone</term>, which I cannot find at the moment—despite searching through all my manuscripts—in which the author informs a certain Baron how he extracted the fixed Salt <note>original symbol: 🜔; represents salt</note> from Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and the volatile salt from Mercury <term>Mercury: In alchemy, this often refers to "Philosophical Mercury," a refined substance rather than common quicksilver</term>. He then successfully joined and processed them.

Some have also derived it from soap, like the old painter in Leipzig; some from urine; some from saliva <note>original: "ex Saliva"</note>; some from metals through a process of glass-making; some from Tin <note>original symbol: ♃; associated with the planet Jupiter</note> and Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>. The late, blessed President Struve <note>original: "Struvin," the feminine form of the surname Struve; likely the wife of a high-ranking official</note> assured me that a man from Hamburg once performed a transmutation <note>original: "tingiret"; to "tinge" or color a base metal, turning it into gold</note> for her using a Tincture <term>Tincture: A concentrated alchemical liquid capable of transforming metals or healing the body</term> prepared from these two. Others [derive it] from dew or a certain frost, as a priest in Thuringia—my heart's trusted friend who also works in this field—has written to me in several confidential letters.

A certain now-deceased Count revealed to me how an artist and alleged Adept named the "frost-stars" as the source for the Universal Work <term>Universal Work: The highest form of alchemy, aiming for a medicine that works on all things</term>. These stars appear in the air as condensed atoms during the hard winters in very cold and harsh weather. He also captured and collected these, obtaining from them a beautiful white earth. We side with those who seek the so-called World-Slime <note>original: "Weltschleim"; a primordial, chaotic substance thought to contain the seeds of all life</note>, the Virginal Adamic Earth <note>original: "Terram virgineam Adamicam"</note>, and the Universal Water...

It highlights the author's personal connections to various practitioners, including a priest and a high-ranking noblewoman.</summary>

<header>Short Report /</header>

...or choose **Saliva** <note>original: "Salivam"</note>, yet not the **common kind that defiles the mouth, but rather our philosophical saliva** <note>original: "vulgarem & os spurcantem, sed philosophicam nostram"</note>.

Before we begin with our own Matter, we wish to **share** <note>original: "communiciren"</note> with all sincere seekers <term>Liebhabern: Literally "lovers" of the art; refers to amateur or aspiring alchemists</term> one or two **secret universal** works. These have been held partly as entirely secret and priceless, and partly have already been **shared** in writings. We do this with the attached earnest plea: that if a seeker and **Artist** <term>Artist: In this context, a practicing chemist or alchemist</term> finds something pleasing therein, he should—out of proper gratitude—attribute it to the present **Author** and report it most faithfully. For if he fails to do so, the curse and misfortune of being an ungrateful person shall not depart from him or his house; on the other hand, the **Author** promises to assist everyone with advice and practical demonstrations <note>original: "Handgriffen"</note> upon request.

### Philalethes’ Path: The True Animation of Mercury
### with Antimony ♁ and Mercury ☿ [twice] <note>original: "Philalethæ Weg: Vera animatio Mercurii cum ♁ ☿ ij." Eirenaeus Philalethes was a famous 17th-century alchemist, often identified as George Starkey.</note>

One allows 6 or 8 **Loth** <term>Loth: A pre-metric unit of weight, roughly 16 grams or half an ounce</term> of beautiful yellow Sulfur to melt gently in a crucible. Then, **gradually** <note>original: "successivè"</note>, one adds half a pound <note>original: "℔ ß."; ℔ is the symbol for a pound, and ß indicates a half</note> of the purest steel filings into it. Once this is done, one adds about one and a quarter pounds of beautiful, long-crystalline Antimony <note>symbol: ♁; "langschüssigen" refers to the needle-like crystal structure of stibnite, a common ore of antimony</note>. (This Antimony must be prepared during beautiful, bright weather and under a clear sky.) Cover the crucible and let it flow together well under a strong fire for half an hour,

# Of the Great Tincture.

...then throw a small glowing coal into it, let it flow a little longer, and then pour it into a casting cone <term>Gießpuckel: A conical iron mold used to separate refined metal from slag</term> greased with tallow <term>Unschlit: Rendered animal fat used as a release agent in molds</term>. This yields a beautiful **Regulus of Antimony** <note>represented by the symbol ♁</note>; separate this from the slag and purify it with **Saltpeter** <note>Nitrum, 🜔</note> and **Tartar** <note>Tartarus, 🜫</note> until it acquires a beautiful star <note>The "Star Regulus" is a crystalline pattern on the surface of purified antimony, which alchemists viewed as a "signature" of its readiness</note>. (This purification is performed approximately four times). From this **prepared Regulus** <note>original: "præparirten Regulo"</note> one now takes 8 **Loth** <term>Loth: A unit of weight, roughly 16 grams</term> and lets it melt together with 16 **Loth** of fine silver. Pour them out together and crush the result into a fine powder. Add to this approximately a 1/4 part of **Saltpeter** <note>🜔</note> and **Vitriol** <note>🜩ris</note> relative to the weight of the powdered **Antimony Regulus** <note>♁</note>. 

Let this heat thoroughly in a fine iron mortar shaped like a bowl. In another clean crucible, take 1 pound <note>℔ j.</note> of highly purified **Mercury** <note>☿</note> and get it quite hot. (Purification can be achieved by turning it into cinnabar, or by sublimating it, or by purging it with **Saltpeter** <note>🜔</note> and **Sal-Ammoniac** <note>🜪</note> and pressing it through a leather cloth). Then pour the heated, powdered **Regulus** <note>♁</note> into it and rub them well together with a wooden pestle. To ensure that nothing turns to dust, sprinkle a little warm water onto it; thus they will accept each other well, the two will unite, and they will become an **amalgam** <note>original: "amalgama"</note>. 

Put this now into a wide **vial** <note>original: "Phiolen"</note> and place it for a few days in a **Water Bath** <note>original: "Baln. M." or Balneum Mariae; a gentle heating method using hot water</note>. Then take the glass out again, let the **amalgam** run into a wooden vessel or bowl, and rub it down thoroughly a second time with the same pestle. And so that this **amalgam** may be even better purified—

# Short Report /

...is done, one rubs it well with pure tartar <term>Weinstein: Potassium bitartrate, used here as a cleaning abrasive</term> and washes it with warm water until all the blackness has come off. This is then refined on a cupel <term>Cappellen: A porous ceramic vessel used to separate precious metals from base metals</term>. Let it flow once more with as much **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁ij</note> (of which one must have plenty in stock) as before, and amalgamate it again with the distilled **Mercury** <note>☿</note> exactly as previously shown, and drive the **Mercury** <note>☿</note> off from it again. This work must be repeated another 5 or 6 times—indeed, even 9 to 10 times (a good friend told me: the more often, the better). Then the **Mercury** <note>☿</note> is perfectly "animated" <note>In alchemy, "animated" mercury is mercury that has been "awakened" or charged with the "metallic soul" of another metal, making it ready for transmutation</note> and can easily transmute **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☽</note> into **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note>.

In this setup, one lets it stand until it acquires a coal-black skin (Note: this is what the philosophers call the **Raven’s Head** <note>original: Raben-Haupt; also known as the *nigredo* stage, signifying the death or dissolution of the old matter</note>). This blackness is now the **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of the oil, or of the philosophers; this occurs within a quarter of a year. 

When it has become black in this way, it is a certain sign of the true **solution** <term>Solution: the chemical and spiritual dissolving of the substances into a primal state</term>. Then one takes the glass out and places it in gentle ash-heat, which must not be hotter than the warmth of the sunshine, so that the **Mercury** <note>original: Mercurius, ☿</note> does not rise. Let it stand therein, and it will take on various colors (which are called the **Peacock’s Tail** <note>original: Pfauen-Schwanz; also known as the *cauda pavonis*, representing the stage where many colors appear before the final purification</note> by the philosophers). 

Finally, it becomes entirely white again, like silver. When it has reached this state, the fire is increased by one degree and maintained until it changes from white into a brownish-red color. ### Short Report

When one part [of the medicine] flows with two parts **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, then one has a **Tincture** <term>Tincture: the final medicinal substance or "Philosopher's Stone" used to transform base metals into gold or silver</term> for **Silver, Mercury, Lead** <note>original symbols: ☽ ☿ ♄</note>, and the other metals. However, if one wishes to **augment** <term>Augmentation: the process of increasing the quantity and potency of the alchemical medicine through repeated cycles</term> such a work infinitely, one takes this red **precipitate** <term>Præcipitat: a solid substance that has settled out of a liquid solution</term> and animates it again with three times as much of the aforementioned **animated mercury**, digesting it as before; in a short time, it will be precipitated into a blood-red mercury, just as it was previously. This can then be **amalgamated** with three times as much animated mercury, and in a short time, it will become a blood-red mercury. In this manner, one can augment the substance infinitely, from which one can always take a portion and project it onto molten gold. (Note: *It does not tinge unless it is tinged* <note>original Latin: *Non tingit, nisi tingatur*; a fundamental alchemical rule stating that the medicine must itself be "colored" or transformed before it can color or transform other metals</note>). In this way, one always has a certain **increase of gold** <note>original Latin: *augmentum Solis*</note>, and thus gold need only be added once; the silver is also recovered. But whoever wishes to work on silver must take silver instead of gold and proceed in the prescribed manner.

A trusted friend also told me this **purification of mercury** for this work: Take one pound of **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and one pound of Mercury, equal parts, well rubbed together; then let it glow <note>heat to a high temperature</note> in a sealed vessel (such as flasks and retorts). After the fire has cooled, take the matter out and mix it with two parts **iron filings**, and then drive the mercury over <note>distill it</note> again and **rectify** it <term>Rectify: to purify a substance through repeated distillation</term>.

I received this process from a highly trusted **practical artist** <term>Real-Artist: a "real" practitioner of the art who works with their hands, as opposed to a theorist or fraud</term> from a famous

# 17
->concerning the Great Tincture.<-

[I have] received from an Imperial City <note>The author refers to receiving a concise alchemical method from a source in an Imperial City (*Reichs-Stadt*), which were self-governing cities within the Holy Roman Empire.</note> [a report] which communicated a very short and yet true **elaboration** <term>elaboration: the technical process of preparing, refining, or "working up" a substance</term>. However, I later found such a **process** described quite honestly and very extensively in the *Chemical Treasury* <note>original Latin: *Ærario Chymico*</note> of my otherwise most beloved **correspondent**, **Dr. David Kelner** <note>David Kelner was a prominent German physician and writer on metallurgy and chemistry active in the late 17th century</note>; which I wish to include here, so that the **curious** <note>In this context, the "curious" (*Curieuse*) refers to the "Sons of Art" or dedicated students of alchemical secrets</note> might receive an even greater light.

### **Universal Process**, as it is to be arranged according to the sense and opinion of **Artephius** and **Philaletha** <note>Two of the most influential authorities in alchemy: Artephius, a legendary medieval figure, and Philaletha (the "Lover of Truth"), the pseudonym of Eirenaeus Philalethes, an 17th-century author whose works were central to Newton and Boyle</note>, **and indeed in the wet way** <note>original Latin: *& quidem in viâ humidâ*; the "wet way" refers to an alchemical method using liquid solvents or acids, often considered safer or more "natural" than the high-heat "dry way"</note>. Namely:

Take <note>original: ℞, the standard symbol for *Recipe*</note> one and a half pounds of thinly laminated steel and half-iron; three **pounds** <note>original: *lb.*</note> and 8 **Loth** <term>Loth: a traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14.5 grams or half an ounce</term> of Hungarian **Antimony** <note>original: *Antimonii*; a semi-metal essential in the "Great Work" for purifying gold and creating the "starry regulus"</note>, which has beautiful yellow-red and blue stripes. Let the steel and iron plates glow well in a crucible; then **successively** <note>original: *successivè*</note> add some of the powdered **antimony** upon them until it has eaten through the iron and they flow well together like water. Upon this, add a further 3 Loth of common **sulfur** mixed with a little of the following **flux-powder**, also adding it bit by bit; and then add the remainder of the flux-powder, which consists of 24 Loth of saltpeter and 8 Loth of **calcined** <term>calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or remove volatile components</term> **tartar** <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate, often used as a flux to help metals melt and separate</note>. When everything is inside, cover the crucible and let it flow well together for one hour; then pour it out into a **casting cone** <term>Gieß-Buckel: a cone-shaped mold used in smelting to allow the heavy metal to settle at the bottom while the lighter slag stays on top</term> that has been slightly warmed and smeared with oil, talc, or wax; then let it cool, pour it out, and knock off the slag—

## Philosophical Preparation of the Sophic Mercury from the Chaos of the Sages, or from the Compound Regulus of Antimony Described Above.

Take: In the Name of GOD, of the previously described and prepared **sublimated Mercury** <note>original: *mercurii sublimati*</note> 2 pounds <note>℔ij</note> and 8 lots <note>a lot or *Loth* is a historical German unit of weight, roughly 1/32 of a pound or 14–17 grams</note>. (The **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring to the alchemical masters of the past</note> call this their **Eagle** <term>Eagle: an alchemical symbol for a substance that has been "elevated" or purified through sublimation</term>, which makes its nest atop the highest mountains—namely, in the **helm** <term>Helm: the top part or "head" of a still where vapors collect and condense</term>. Through **natural magic** <note>original: *magiam naturalem*; the practice of using the inherent properties of natural substances to achieve transformations</note>, this eagle radically unlocks the **Regulus or Chaos of the Sages** <term>Chaos Sophorum: the raw, unrefined "First Matter" from which the philosophers' stone is made</term> and resolves it into the true **Mercurial Water**, by its own mercurial nature).

Grind this mercury very fine in a glass or marble mortar. Add to it 1 pound and 8 lots of the aforementioned **four-fold compound Regulus** <term>Regulus: the purified metallic heart of a mineral, here referring to the antimony compound described in earlier sections</term>, which must be perfectly ground. Also add 8 lots of the gold and silver precipitate mentioned previously. Grind everything well together; however, one must not linger but must be quick so that it is soon well-mixed and placed into a strong, high-quality glass **retort** <term>Retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</term>.

The retort must be of such a size that the material occupies or fills barely a fourth part of it. A ground glass stopper that seals and fits well should be ready, so that one may soon **seal it** <note>original: *verlutire*; "to lute" or hermetically seal a vessel to prevent the escape of vapors</note>. This is done first with a good pig's bladder which has been soaked in melted **isinglass** <term>Isinglass: a high-quality collagen/glue made from the dried swim bladders of fish, particularly sturgeon</term> mixed with brandy; wrap this around the stopper several times and let it dry on its own. Afterwards, seal it with a good, firm stone-lute <note>original: *Stein-Kütte*; a strong cement used to seal alchemical apparatus against high heat</note>, or

When mixing and placing the matter into the **retorts** <term>Retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</term>, one must take great care against the smell and vapor. By binding the nose and mouth, and also by holding one's breath, one protects oneself so that it brings no harm. For the moment the mixing occurs and the substances touch one another, they are accustomed to react violently together, and in a short time, thick mists rise from it in the retort and circulate within.) The fire of the **precipitated Mercury** <note>original: *mercurii præcipit.*</note> and the **red servant** <term>Red Servant: a metaphorical term for the masculine, solar, or sulfurous principle hidden within the metal</term> hidden within—namely the oil of **Venus** <note>represented by the symbol ♀; refers to copper</note>—will awaken and activate the hidden inner fire of the previously mentioned **compound golden Regulus of Antimony** <note>represented by the symbol ♁ii; "Regulus" refers to the purified metallic heart of the mineral</note> as its equal. This occurs in such a way that it begins to distill without the addition of any external fire; indeed, it becomes almost red-hot from the reaction. Finally, such mists resolve into a moisture which flows down the sides of the glass like white milk, and in a short time, they merge into a **liquid** <note>original: *liquorem*</note> which covers the entire matter. Thus, the **primordial fundamental moisture** of the **Sophic Chaos** <note>original: *Chai Sophici*</note>—which had lain motionless and, as it were, dead within that so tightly locked **metallic Chaos of the Sages** <note>original: *Chao Sophorum metallico*</note>—is elicited in a magnetic fashion by its own kind, as fire is drawn out by fire. When such action is over and the resulting heat has subsided, a beautiful salt, similar to **diamonds**, will attach itself to the sides or walls <note>original: *lateribus oder parietibus*</note> of the glass, and the liquid [will seek] the earth with the highest desire,

The first degree of fire must be given in such a way that the mists in the glass are completely clear and circulate gently; but if they appear dark and move quickly or nimbly within it, it is a sign that the fire has been given too strongly. Therefore, this must be changed and lessened immediately, which can be done by plugging the vents or registers of the furnace; otherwise, the internal fire would be excited too strongly, so that the glass also runs the risk of shattering if it gets out of hand. But in the aforementioned circulation, there is no danger if the fire is circulated in the manner described.

In such circulation, the spirit now works by continuous operation <note>original: *continua operatione*</note> into the body <note>original: *Corpus*</note> of the Sophic Regulus <term>Sophic Regulus: A purified metallic mass, often derived from antimony, considered a "royal" starting material for the Great Work</term>, until the predominating Sulfur <note>original: *prædominirende Sulphur*</note> within it is killed, and has fundamentally <note>original: *radicitus*</note> resolved and freed itself in its inner center <note>original: *Centro*</note>, so that the pure might separate from the impure in the Sophic manner <note>original: *purum Sophico more ab impuro*</note>. This must not happen violently <note>original: *violenter*</note> at all, but softly or gently <note>original: *blandè sive leniter*</note>, following the instruction of Nature, which loves mildness and sweetness in all its operations <note>original: *quæ in omnibus suis operationibus lenitatem ac suavitatem amat*</note>; for it will not allow itself to be forced against its implanted nature.

During the time of digestion <note>original: *digestion*</note> and circulation <note>original: *Circulation*</note>, clear crystalline streaks <note>original: *striæ*</note> or droplets <note>original: *guttulæ*</note>—like teardrops or dewdrops—will constantly flow down the walls <note>original: *parietibus*</note> of the retorts, provided the work is rightly [governed].

### OF THE GREAT TINCTURE.

For if this did not occur, no actual dissection of the Philosophical Work would ever take place, nor would the *matter* <note>original: *materia*</note> allow itself to be *resolved* <note>original: *resolviren*</note> and brought into its *principles* <note>original: *principia*; the fundamental components of mercury, sulfur, and salt</note>.

At this point, the inner, fixed Gold- and Silver-Spirit—which lay quite dead and without effect in the *body* <note>original: *corpore*</note>—is, as it were, awakened and enters into a *spiritual* <note>original: *spirituale*</note> effect. From the fixed, a spiritual and volatile being is created, which thereafter, through its own nature, can *radically dissolve* <note>original: *radicaliter solviren*</note> the solidified bodies of gold and silver and make them spiritual and volatile like itself. This could otherwise never happen if it had not previously partaken of such a volatile nature. *Hence the axiom: Make the fixed volatile, and the volatile fixed, etc.* <note>original: *Unde axioma: Fac fixum volatile, & volatile fixum, &c.*</note>

Such *treatment* <note>original: *tractation*</note>, however, can hardly be completed in less than fifteen to sixteen weeks, for it cannot be rushed at all; and if it does not pass perfectly through its own and nature-appropriate *putrefaction*, which is the master key of the...

### Short Report/

...of the whole Chemical Art, which one must observe very precisely and well if a successful progress in this work is to follow. For if the *Chaos* <note>original: *Chaos*; the disorganized primordial matter</note> is not correctly *dissolved* <note>original: *solviret*</note> here, no true *separation of the principles* <note>original: *Separatio principiorum*; the division of the matter into its fundamental components of mercury, sulfur, and salt</note> can be hoped for or expected. When all this, as previously mentioned, is well attended to and has occurred, a white crystalline salt will appear and be seen on top of the matter. This will then be the true sign—and indeed it truly is—that the *putrefaction* <note>original: *putrefaction*; the process of breakdown or decay</note> has completed its full *philosophical operations* <note>original: *operationes philosophicas*</note>. At this you may rejoice from the heart, being assured that you have worked correctly. 

Then, let your *athanor* <term>Athanor: A specialized furnace used by alchemists, designed to maintain a constant, steady heat for long periods, often featuring a fuel hopper to keep the fire burning for days without intervention.</term> go out and cool. After opening the *joints* <note>original: *juncturen*</note> or mouth—but beware of the smell, for it is not very good—place the *retorts* <note>original: *Retorten*; glass vessels with long, downward-pointing necks used for distilling liquids</note> into a *sand bath* <note>original: *Sand-Cappell*; a container filled with sand used to heat a glass vessel evenly</note>. *Seal* <note>original: *lutire*</note> a flask to it and secure it with the *lute* <note>original: *Luto*; an airtight sealing paste or clay used to prevent vapors from escaping</note> as best as possible. When it is well dried, give it fire *gradually* <note>original: *gradatim*</note> and *distill* <note>original: *destillire*</note> it over gently. It will pass over like a mist, which transforms into a *liquid* <note>original: *Liquorem*</note> and crystalline salt. It *congeals* <note>original: *coaguliret*</note> in the cold, but at the gentlest warmth, it flows like an oil. This is the true *Coagulated Water of Saturn* <note>original: *Aqua coagulata Saturni*; in this context, "Saturn" usually refers to lead or a heavy metallic essence</note>, in which the rays of the Sun and the Moon are contained, concentrated in entirely spiritual power and effect. It will also be of a somewhat high color. Drive it so long, until nothing more passes over—

### A Philosophical Proposition for the Tincture.

Here you must now at the beginning well consider within yourself whether you wish to set the composition or Tincture for the Red or the White <note>The "Red" work aims for the transmutation into gold, while the "White" work aims for silver</note>. Take 2 parts of this, to which you should add 1 part of well-prepared <term>Gold Calx: gold reduced to a fine, oxide-like powder through calcination</term> on its own, which looks like a marigold yellow or a scarlet red; place this into a glass (which must only be filled to the fourth part), seal this well at the top in the philosophical manner <note>original: *philosophico more*; referring to a hermetic seal, often created by melting the glass neck shut</note>, and then set it into digestion to circulate. Give it a very gentle fire at first, and it will dissolve the gold calx in a magnetic way, as its own kind. ## of the great Tincture.

# A Brief Account

### of the Great Tincture

...work, provided only that the degree of fire is cautiously observed and administered. When the colors have followed one another in the correct order, it finally turns into a dry powder, becoming fixed and white as snow, which is the true, permanent Whiteness <note>The *Albedo* stage: the point where the matter has been purified and is no longer volatile</note>. Once you see this, you should rejoice and heartily thank **God** for the grace bestowed upon you. Then you no longer need to fear so greatly that you might easily over-hasten or ruin the work by the degree of fire, since you have then overcome the greatest danger that could befall you in this process.

Nevertheless, one must likewise not rush through the other subsequent degrees of fire. Then, this Whiteness will gradually fade and turn into a yellow color, which afterwards shifts into a redness, until it reaches the highest and most fixed Ruby-Redness <note>The final *Rubedo* stage, signifying the completion of the Philosopher's Stone</note>. When this has appeared, you may maintain a fairly strong degree of fire so that it may completely <term>fix: the alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat</term>; then you have brought the work to a desired and happy conclusion, where 1 grain of this Medicine will tinge <note>original: *tingiren*; to transmute or "dye" a base metal into a noble one</note> 1,000 parts of the imperfect metals into the most permanent gold <note>original: $\odot$; the alchemical symbol for gold</note>. 

This Medicine, however, does not tinge any sooner unless it is fermented and combined with 3 parts of fine gold, all of which becomes a brittle red gold or a fragile mass; then, one part will tinge a thousand parts.

## Fermentation or Augmentation of the
## Tincture.

This prepared, fixed tincture can be augmented so that its powers and virtues increase more and more, and it may multiply itself infinitely within them, if one is able to bring it to the seventh fermentation. However, this rarely happens because of its exceedingly great subtlety and fiery nature; for in the end, it becomes a pure and penetrating *fire* that cannot be contained in any glass <note>The author suggests that at its highest potency, the substance becomes so energetic it would shatter or melt a glass vessel</note>. But when it reaches the 5th Fermentation and is augmented, one may be satisfied or rest there, for it can then sufficiently extend and demonstrate its effect, so that one will be more than well-satisfied with it. For this great mystery, mortal man can never offer enough thanks and praise to his **GOD**.

Take 1 ½ parts of the prepared fixed tincture, and 3 parts of a well-prepared gold-calx <term>gold-calx: a fine powder produced by chemically dissolving and then precipitating gold</term>, or of the finest thin-beaten gold leaf. Put these together into a vial, seal it hermetically <note>original: *sigillire sie hermeticè*; to seal a glass vessel airtight by melting the neck</note>, and place it in an <term>athanor: a furnace used by alchemists designed to maintain a uniform heat over long periods</term>. Then apply a very gentle fire, and it will dissolve <note>original: *solviren*</note> once more and enter into a state of volatility <note>original: *Volatilität*; the ability of the substance to evaporate or rise as vapor</note>; it will also reach putrefaction <term>putrefaction: the "Nigredo" or black stage of the "Great Work" where the matter decays and turns black, signifying the death of the old form</term> anew, becoming as black as black pitch, and then pass through all the colors until it reaches the highest redness again. ### Short Report

Fire must be governed and guided through all degrees <note>original: *per omnes gradus*</note>, just as happened in the first treatment <note>original: *Tractation*</note>. 

In this second rotation or augmentation <term>augmentation: the process of increasing the power or quantity of the alchemical stone</term>, the work will reach its perfection and fixation <note>original: *perfection und Fixation*</note> twelve weeks sooner than happened in the first preparation. Once this augmentation has come to an end again, one may take out half and keep it for use; however, the other half should be applied <note>original: *adhibiren*</note> to further augmentation. This occurs in the exact same proportion and weight, and with the same treatment of the fire. It will likewise enter into putrefaction <term>putrefaction: a stage where the substance typically turns black, representing the "death" of the matter before its purification</term> just as occurred in the other rotation or augmentation, but it will reach fixation and perfection several weeks sooner. 

In the 4th and 5th augmentation, it will again reach its end several weeks earlier, so that in the final stage, it runs completely through all the colors and operations <note>original: *Operationes*</note> within a few weeks. However, each time, for such a prepared tincture—namely one and a half parts of it—three parts of gold-calx <term>gold-calx: gold reduced to a fine powder through chemical dissolution and precipitation</term> and its thin leaves must be taken and mixed with six parts of its own mercurial water <term>mercurial water: a specialized alchemical solvent, often a purified or "animated" mercury</term>, and be dissolved anew <note>original: *de novo dissolviret*</note>. In this, there is nothing more to observe than that the degree of the fire is accurately administered and maintained at all times according to the prescribed manner. Thus, one will obtain such a treasure that nothing earthly in the world can be compared to it.

If you wish, however, to work the tincture upon silver—

### Some Notes on This Process.

When one has prepared this **gold-making mercurial water** <note>original: *Aquam Mercurialem aurificam*</note> to such an extent that the seven **re-distillations** <term>re-distillations: also called "eagles" (Aquilæ); the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid over its solid residue to concentrate its power</term> over the **dead head** <term>dead head: *caput mortuum*; the solid remains or "earth" left in the flask after distillation</term> are completed and flown off, it is necessary to extract the fixed **salt of metals** <note>original: *Sal Metallorum*</note> still contained within the remaining small amount of earth using **distilled water or distilled vinegar**. However, the vinegar must stand over it for one or two days in gentle heat, and thus **digest** <term>digest: to expose a substance to steady, gentle heat to encourage a chemical transformation or extraction</term> together; then it must be **filtered**, and the **solvent** <term>solvent: or *menstruum*; the liquid used to dissolve a solid substance</term>—be it vinegar or water—must be gently **distilled off** until a **crust** forms. Then stop and let it **crystallize in a cold place**. In this way, you will obtain a most beautiful fixed salt, which can be **dissolved** and **crystallized** once more in vinegar so that it becomes quite pure, bright, and translucently clear.

Take these obtained crystals and pour the **mercurial water** upon them; let them **dissolve** in gentle heat, and some **dross** <note>original: *feces*; the insoluble impurities or waste</note> will still remain behind. Pour the **solution** off from this...

**Artephius** <note>a legendary alchemist, traditionally identified as a 12th-century writer, though many works under his name appeared later; he was famous for supposedly living over a thousand years</note> is also said to have **prepared** this **solvent** solely through the "rays" of gold and silver with the addition of a **simple regulus of antimony** <term>regulus of antimony: the purified metallic heart of the antimony ore, named "regulus" or "little king" because it reacts with gold</term>. In this way, he is likewise said to [have obtained] a very noble **solvent or gold-making mercurial water**...

## Second Process.

### Shall be arranged, according to the opinion of Philalethes, in the **dry way**. <term>dry way: *via sicca*; a method of alchemical transformation involving high heat, crucibles, and furnaces, rather than liquid-based dissolution</term>

Whoever reads and well considers the writings of **Philalethes** <note>referring to Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist whose identity remained a mystery; his works were essential reading for scholars like Robert Boyle and Isaac Newton</note> with a good understanding and a solid foundation in the truth, shall find that he writes of two ways in which the **Great Tincture** <note>the "Great Tincture" is another name for the transformative power of the Philosophers' Stone</note> or **Philosophers' Stone** <term>Philosophers' Stone: *Lapis Philosophorum*; the ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to transmute base metals into gold and act as a universal medicine</term> can be prepared and finished; namely, through the **wet way** and the **dry way**. However, he describes these very obscurely and mixes one into the other, so that even a very well-practiced person must pay close and careful attention to this if they wish to distinguish them correctly. Although **experience** <note>original: *Experienz*</note> afterwards testifies that he has not written wrongly, because one way cannot be well handled without the preparation of the other.

Out of love and for the sake of the seeker, I have already partly untied this difficult knot of doubt in the preceding process (**faithfully** <note>original: *fideliter*</note> describing the **method of treatment** <note>original: *modum tractandi*</note> therein); and I will further provide sincere **disclosure** <note>original: *communication*; in an alchemical context, this means the "passing on" of secret knowledge</note> here as to how, specifically, the **handling** <note>original: *tractatio*</note> in the **dry way** must be established. Therefore, may you follow this faithful and well-intentioned instruction, alongside diligent prayer, and continue tirelessly in the work; then you will undoubtedly see and attain a successful outcome in this matter.

# Short Report

However, it is unnecessary for me to repeat here what I have already described in detail and clearly in the first process. For the **dry way** <term>dry way: *via sicca*; a method of alchemical transformation involving high heat and crucibles rather than liquid solvents</term> is nothing other than knowing how to unite the bodies of **Gold** <note>original: *Solis*</note> and **Silver** <note>original: *Lunæ*</note> with **purified running mercury** <note>original: *mercurio currente purgato*</note> and the **mercury of antimony** <note>original: *mercurio antimonii*</note>, together with the fire of **Iron** <note>original: *Martis*</note> in a **philosophical manner** <note>original: *more philosophico*</note>, in a **running form** <note>original: *formâ currenti*</note>. From this, one must know how to prepare the **doubled mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium duplicatum*</note>, so that from this a **mercury** is created which is then called by the true Philosophers and Adepts, with all right and reason, the **Philosophers' Mercury** <term>Philosophers' Mercury: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; the refined, "living" essence of mercury required for the Great Work, as opposed to common quicksilver</term>. And indeed it is truly so, for in it all **virtues** <note>powers or essential properties</note> and **qualities** are found concentrated in a **spiritual way** <note>original: *spirituali modo*</note>. Through the Philosophical treatments and operations according to Nature, **common mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius vulgi*</note> is completely cleansed and separated from its inherent arsenical moisture and combustible, foul, stinking sulfurous nature, and thus is changed and transformed into a new **Transparent Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium Diaphanum*</note>.

Take, in the name of God, the **gold-making mercurial water** <note>original: *Aquam mercurialem aurificam*</note> described in the preceding process and prepared with the greatest care, and pour it into pure distilled **rainwater** that has been separated from all earthiness. This should be contained in a large, spacious **glass flask** <term>glass flask: *Glaß-Kolben*; a chemical vessel, here noted as being cut in half to create a wide-mouthed container</term> which has been cut off halfway. # Of the Great Tincture

### Short Report

...to become a **running Mercury** <note>original: *lauffenden Mercurium*</note>. For this **Chaos** <term>Chaos: in alchemy, this refers to the raw, unrefined "prime matter" before it is organized into a specific substance</term>, through such philosophical treatment and the first **Putrefaction** <term>Putrefaction: a stage where the matter decays and breaks down, traditionally turning black, to allow for its spiritual "rebirth"</term> it has undergone, has become an entirely new, regenerated, and as it were, a completely spiritual body. 

And it will never again be brought to a true, indissoluble union with the still red, corporeal, and—for the philosophical operation—unprepared **raw gold-making martial regulus of antimony** <note>original: *regulo antimonii martiato aurifico crudo*</note> and **common running Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio vulgari currente*</note> through their imagined **amalgamations** <term>amalgamation: the process of mixing mercury with other metals to create a soft alloy</term> and so-called **rotations** <term>rotation: the repeated heating and cooling or cycling of a substance to increase its potency</term>. 

For in the Chaos as well as in the **common Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio vulgi*</note>, there is far too much arsenical and harmful-sulphuric essence present, which cannot be so easily separated from them—namely, from the **Chaos of the Wise** <note>original: *Chao Sophorum*</note>, that is, the **gold-making martial regulus of antimony** <note>original: *Regulo scilicet antimonii martiali aurifico*</note> and the **running Mercury** <note>original: *mercurio currente*</note>. Rather, it is always found mixed and dragging along like an **unrighteous husband** <note>This is a metaphor for a chemical bond that is impure or restrictive</note>, thereby hindering the **entry** <note>original: *ingressuum*; the ability of the alchemical spirit to penetrate the metal</note>, so that the Mercuries cannot unite intimately in **amalgamation** <note>original: *amalgamatione*</note>. 

Even if it happens through manifold repetitions, the true **Double Mercury** <term>Double Mercury: *Mercurius duplicatus*; a highly refined mercury that has been joined with a metallic "sulfur" or soul, making it twice as powerful as common mercury</term> will still not come of it; instead, it remains dead, as before. Thus, it cannot naturally **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> gold and silver in their innermost **center** <note>original: *centro*</note>, nor bring them to a volatile Mercury like itself, unless it is [acted upon] by the fiery **Sulphur of Mars** <note>original: *Sulphure Martis*; the active, spiritual principle extracted from iron</note> and the heated rays of the **Sun** <note>original: *Solis*; here referring to Gold</note> and the again-tempering light of the **Moon** <note>original: *Lunæ*; here referring to Silver</note> through Mer-

# Of the Great Tincture

...**Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> has been properly animated and transformed and made into the **duplicate Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio duplicato*</note> and the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note>, then it is permitted from above to exercise such power that it can turn gold and silver into a uniform **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> along with itself. In the correctly and philosophically prepared **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note> are found two types of powers and singular properties; namely, one that makes **fixed** <note>original: *fix*</note> <term>fixed: in alchemy, a substance that is stable in fire and does not evaporate or change</term> and one that makes volatile, just as there is the property of **Solar Mercury** <note>original: *Solarischen Mercurii*</note> <term>Solar Mercury: mercury that has been joined with the essence of gold, often considered the "inner soul" of the metal</term> and of **common running Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii vulgaris currentis*</note>. One of these is a fixed spiritual body, but the other is a stable spirit, because neither of the two is destructible; rather, they always remain together as one kind, according to the well-known **axiom** <note>original: *Axiomate*</note>: **Make the volatile fixed, and the fixed volatile, and vice versa** <note>original: *Fac volatile fixum, & fixum volatile, & vice versa*</note>. However, here in this measured philosophical **operation** <note>original: *Operation*</note> and **treatment** <note>original: *Tractation*</note>, they are united, whereas previously the gold was **fixed** <note>original: *fix*</note> but the **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> was volatile, and ultimately both become fixed together.

One must take into no small **consideration** <note>original: *Consideration*</note> what this **united force or conjunction** <note>original: *vis unita vel conjunctio*</note> <term>conjunction: the alchemical "marriage" or union of two opposite substances, such as "fixed" gold and "volatile" mercury</term> is capable of achieving. If one rightly considers the gold's exceedingly great **fixity** <note>original: *fixität*</note> and fire-stability—which it has received from the upper **stars** <note>original: *astris*</note> and **subterranean fire** <note>original: *igne subterraneo*</note> <term>subterranean fire: the central heat within the earth that alchemists believed "cooked" base metals into gold over centuries</term> through many, indeed perhaps thousands of years of **generation** <note>original: *generation*</note>—one sees what a **fixed** <note>original: *fixes*</note> and supremely stable being it will become in regard to **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>, which is the most volatile, subtle, and penetrating spirit. **Also** <note>original: *Item*</note> / what it

## Brief Report

...for a spiritual quality—and in consideration of the **golden sulfur** <term>golden sulfur: the "soul" or active combustible principle extracted from gold, believed to give the alchemical medicine its power</term>, as it is drawn from the **martial regulus of antimony** <term>martial regulus of antimony: a metallic form of antimony produced by refining it with iron (Mars)</term> and **Gold** <note>original: *Sole*</note>—it shall receive and obtain a multiplying and **tingeing** <term>tingeing: the ability of an alchemical substance to "color" or transform base metals into gold or silver</term> quality.

Now, to proceed to the process itself, let the lover of the art take one part of the philosophically prepared **Magistery** <term>Magistery: a master-medicine or highly refined substance</term>, namely, the powder finely and subtly ground in a glass mortar. And take six parts of the very well purified and somewhat already animated **running mercury** <note>original: *☿ii currentis*</note> recovered in the previously described process, which has beforehand been washed again with purified **sal ammoniac** <term>sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, used for cleaning and preparing metals</term> and distilled vinegar, and finally well washed with water, and pressed through a chamois leather. Then place the white powder, or **Sophic Magistery** <note>original: *Magisterium Sophicum*</note>, into a marble or glass mortar, and let it become warm with the same over a very gentle charcoal fire.

When it has become so warm that one can hardly bear a finger in it, then add the **running mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium currentem*</note>—likewise previously made warm enough that it is about to begin to smoke—to the aforementioned powder. Lift the mortar from the fire and set it on a previously warmed spot, and begin to **amalgamate** <term>amalgamate: to blend mercury with another substance into a soft, paste-like mixture</term> it with a fairly broad club or **pestle** <note>original: *Pistill*</note> smoothly turned from hard wood. Continue this with constant rubbing until the fine powder is entirely lost and enters into the **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>, which usually happens within several hours.

### Of the Great Tincture.

Then stop; take the **amalgam** <term>amalgam: a mixture or blend, specifically one involving mercury and another metal</term> neatly out of the mortar, wash it well, and clean it first with vinegar, and afterwards thoroughly with pure spring water, until the water comes away from it entirely pure, bright, and clear. Then dry the water still clinging to it with a sponge or blotting paper; place it in a small flask in a warm place, so that all moisture evaporates completely from it and it becomes thoroughly dry. 

Then place this amalgam of yours for 14 days and nights into the **athanor** <term>athanor: a furnace used by alchemists designed to provide a steady, constant heat over long periods</term> in gentle **digestion** <term>digestion: the process of heating a substance slowly to promote chemical change or maturation</term> (the glass must be very well sealed); thus both **mercuries** <note>original: *Mercurii* — referring to the metallic mercury and the philosophical principle extracted from the gold</note> will, during the time of digestion, unite **amicably** <note>original: *amicabiliter*</note> bit by bit, and enter into a **radical solution** <term>radical solution: a fundamental dissolving where the substances are merged at their most basic level</term>. This placement can be done in a good glass **retort** <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck, used for distillation</term> with a ground-in stopper. 

After this 14-day digestion, open the glass and place it in a **sand bath** <term>sand bath: a container filled with sand heated from below, used to provide even heat to a glass vessel</term>; give it fire **gradually** <note>original: *gradatim*</note>. Attach a receiver filled with clear water, reaching up to the neck of the retort, yet in such a way that it still retains some air, and distill your **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> over **in running form** <note>original: *in Forma currente*</note>. 

When this is done, let the fire go out; take the remaining **matter** or **earth** <note>original: *Materiam* or *Terram*</note> out of the retort, grind it small and delicate again like a flour. Take the mercury that has been distilled over and purified of all watery moisture, and warm it again in a **crucible** <term>crucible: a container made of ceramic or other high-heat resistant material used for melting metals</term>, and amalgamate the powder with it, as mentioned above, also in the mortar...

-># Concerning the Great Tincture<-

# Short Report

...parts of the sphere <note>referring to the round body of the glass vessel, often called the "Philosophical Egg"</note> must always remain empty, so that the material might circulate properly and not easily burst. The neck of the vessel must not be more than 6 to 7 inches high, and it must be sealed very carefully in the philosophical manner <term>philosophical manner: hermetically sealing a glass vessel by melting the neck shut to ensure it is airtight</term> <note>original: *philosophico more*</note>.

The reason why this proportion of weight—3 parts of Sophic Mercury <term>Sophic Mercury: the purified, "living" essence of mercury, the primary solvent in the Great Work</term> <note>original: *Mercurii Sophici*</note> and 1 part of the Ferment of the Sun or Moon <term>Ferment of the Sun or Moon: purified gold or silver used as a "seed" to guide the mercury toward becoming a Tincture</term> <note>original: *Fermenti Solis vel Lunæ*</note>—is taken here in the Composition <term>Composition: the critical stage where the purified ingredients are combined to begin the transmutation process</term>, is because in the "running" Sophic Mercury <note>original: *Mercurio Sophico currente*; referring to mercury in its active, mobile state</note> there is already a greater coagulative or fixative power <term>fixative power: the ability to make a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</term> <note>original: *vis coagulativa sive fixativa*</note> present than in the liquid Sophic Mercury; and so that it can dissolve <note>original: *resolviren*</note> and activate <note>original: *activiren*</note> the Gold ferment <note>symbolized by the solar glyph $\odot$</note> added to it within its center, or bring it to activity <note>original: *ad activitatem*</note>, all the sooner.

Therefore, place your vials <note>original: *Phiolen*</note> filled with the Sophic Mercury and the added Ferment of the Sun or Moon into your Athanor <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed by alchemists to maintain a constant, steady temperature for weeks or months</term> or philosophical furnace. Three to four vials can certainly be placed in one cupel <term>cupel: here used to describe the tray or sand-bath container inside the furnace</term> <note>original: *Capell*</note>, provided that enough space <note>original: *Spacium*</note> remains around each one so that the ash or sand can lie evenly around them.

At the beginning, provide a very gentle degree of fire or warmth, roughly equal to that of a brooding hen <note>a standard alchemical metaphor for gentle, life-giving heat, approximately 100°F or 38°C</note>, and by no means force the work with strong heat if you wish to work successfully. For a single coal—as the Philosophers <term>Philosophers: the historical masters and authors of alchemical texts</term> faithfully warn—can ruin and destroy your treasure, upon which you have placed such great hope and confidence. [the] heat will be excited, it will begin its **philosophical calcinations** <term>philosophical calcinations: a process of reducing a substance to a fine powder or essence using gentle heat rather than harsh, destructive burning</term> <note>original: *philosophicas calcinationes*</note> into the so tightly sealed, compact **gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> and **silver** <note>symbol: ☽</note>, and will **continue inwardly** <note>original: *intrinsece continuiren*</note> until it has opened or unlocked the same in its center, and has made it into a volatile **mercury** <term>mercury: here referring to the "Sophic Mercury," the liquid, transformative essence of the metal</term> <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> like itself. Finally, after the appearance of all sorts of colors playing among one another, it will begin to turn into a blackness, and continue therein until it comes to the blackest of blacks <note>this refers to the "Nigredo" or putrefaction stage, the necessary first death of the material in the Great Work</note>.

However, if during the operation, before the blackness, a bright red color should show itself—which lasts and remains for several days—and which the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> call the **Poppy-Rose Redness** <note>original: *Klatsche Rosen Röthe*</note>, this is a certain sign that you have applied the degree of fire too strongly at the beginning of its flowering, and have burned it before its time. Thus, you will never bring your work to the desired end. But if such redness stays away and does not appear, then be of good cheer and comfort yourself that you have given and governed the degree of fire correctly. For in the philosophical work, no other **rules** <note>original: *Regulen*</note> and **laws** <note>original: *Leges*</note> can be prescribed or given than that one must be guided by the work itself, namely as it shows itself in the glass and as ocular evidence <note>direct observation</note> teaches you; the **artist** <note>original: *Artiste*; the alchemical practitioner</note> must be guided by this and know how to apply the degrees of fire. In short, **judgment** <note>original: *Judicium*</note> or understanding must be the best teacher and guide in this. When you, however, after the preceding colors...

# Short Report /

you obtain the proper blackness, and it remains stable (for such will last over 100 days and stand unchanged), and then begins to turn into a yellowish color, then you are assured of the right and true **Solution** <term>Solution: the chemical or mystical dissolving of a solid substance into a liquid state</term> <note>original: *Solution*</note> in this work, and certain of a successful outcome, whereas before you were still hovering between fear and hope. Also, you no longer have to fear as much that you might ruin your work by some stronger degree of fire, because from now on it can withstand more heat; nevertheless, good foresight and caution must still be used. Accordingly, this yellow will turn into an orange-colored yellowness <note>original: *Pomeranzen farbige Gilbe*</note>, which, after it has stood for a while, will turn into whiteness and gradually become whiter and whiter, until at last the proper, true whiteness fully appears, which nothing can surpass in brilliance. The **sons of the art** <term>sons of the art: a traditional term for practitioners of alchemy, or those initiated into its secrets</term> <note>original: *filii artis*</note> long heartily to attain this; then the **philosophical work** <note>the "Great Work" or Magnum Opus of alchemy</note> has, by the grace of God, progressed so far that such a fixed or stable whiteness should be able to **tincture** <term>tincture: the alchemical power to transform the nature and color of a base metal into a noble one</term> <note>original: *tingiren*</note> a large portion of the imperfect metals into silver. However, one does not do this, but must continue by increasing the degree of fire; then it will begin to change again and become yellowish, though this does not last long at all, but soon hastens bit by bit toward the **redness** <note>the final stage of the work, known as the *Rubedo*</note>, until it reaches its highest degree, and

# Of the Great Tincture.

...will have transformed itself, through the increase of the fire, into a deep red, fixed, and fire-resistant powder. Then you have completed the first **Rotation** <term>Rotation: a cyclical repetition of alchemical processes—dissolving and solidifying—intended to purify and strengthen the substance</term> <note>original: *Rotation*</note> and attained the **Eagle** <term>Eagle: an alchemical symbol for sublimation, where the "volatile" spirit is raised up</term> <note>original: *Aquilam*</note>, having reached **more-than-perfection** <note>original: *plus-quam perfection*</note> through this hermetic operation. One will not, however, perceive any visible **Sublimation** <term>Sublimation: the process of heating a substance until it vaporizes and then collecting the purified solid that forms upon cooling</term> or **Circulation** <term>Circulation: the continuous movement of spirits within a sealed vessel, mimicking the natural cycle of evaporation and condensation</term> in this work, as often occurs in the "wet path" <note>the 'wet path' involves liquid solutions, whereas this 'dry path' focuses on high-heat crucible work</note>; rather, it must happen imperceptibly and **intrinsically** <note>original: *intrinsecè*; occurring within the internal structure of the matter</note>, while it has turned into a black dust and powder. By this, nothing else is understood by the **Philosopher** <note>a traditional term for an alchemical author or practitioner</note> than a perfect elevation of nature within the inward essence of the **Sophic Mercury** <term>Sophic Mercury: the "Mercury of the Wise," a purified, philosophical substance believed to be the root of all metals</term> <note>original: *Mercurii sophici*</note>.

Within this deep red, fixed earth, the philosophical fire lies hidden, which the **Philosophers** call the "natural fire." This consists of common gold and the golden **Sulfur of Mars** <term>Sulfur of Mars: the soul or active chemical essence derived from iron (Mars)</term> <note>original: *Schwefel Martis*</note>, which lay hidden in our **Chaos or Starry Regulus of Gold-making Antimony** <term>Regulus of Antimony: a metallic form of antimony, often appearing with a star-like crystalline pattern on its surface when purified</term> <note>original: *Chao sive Regulo Antimonii stellato aurifico*</note>. Through the operation of **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>, however, it was drawn out from the same; and Mercury itself—by the power of these fires, which are of exactly the same nature as those found in **Mercury** <image-desc>alchemical symbol for Mercury</image-desc>—has **coagulated** <term>Coagulate: the process of thickening or solidifying a liquid into a stable mass</term> and has also been transformed into the nature of a metallic fire or sulfur. In this way, all the moisture of the Mercury has been turned into dryness and heat by these united fires. The red **fixed** <note>original: *Fix*; meaning stable and resistant to evaporation by fire</note> earth must now be **elevated** again through the second **Rotation or Eagle** <note>original: *Rotation s. [seu] aquilam*</note> and refined in power and virtue...

# Short Report

...will be accomplished; and this occurs when you take one part of the substance—while it is still warm—and four parts of the **Sophic Mercury** <term>Sophic Mercury: the "Mercury of the Wise," a purified, philosophical substance believed to be the root of all metals</term> <note>original: *Mercurio sophico*</note>, which you must have in supply and were previously commanded to set aside. **Amalgamate** <term>Amalgamate: to blend or alloy a metal with mercury</term> <note>original: *amalgamirest*</note> them together in a warm glass mortar. This union will occur quite readily due to their inherent sympathy. However, it will leave behind some black earth and, as if through a **vomit** <note>original: *Vomitum*; a term used for the spontaneous expulsion of impurities during the alchemical process</note>, will cast it out from itself. This residue is called **dead and damned earth** <note>original: *terra mortua & damnata*; the useless dross or salt left over after the "soul" of the matter has been extracted</note> by the **Philosopher** <note>a traditional term for an alchemical author or practitioner</note> in this work.

Then, place it into a suitable **phial glass** <note>original: *Phiol-Glaß*</note>, **seal** <note>original: *sigillire*; likely a hermetic seal made by melting the glass tip</note> it well, and place it back into the philosophical furnace. Direct the heat to the same degree as was done in the first **treatment** <note>original: *Tractation*</note>. In this way, it will once again pass through its proper colors until it returns to the highest redness. At that point, through every **rotation** <term>Rotation: a cyclical repetition of alchemical processes—dissolving and solidifying—intended to purify and strengthen the substance</term> or **eagle** <term>Eagle: an alchemical symbol for sublimation, where the "volatile" spirit is raised up</term> <note>original: *Aquilam*</note>, this fixed, red **Tincture-Earth** <note>original: *Tinctur-Erde*</note> will **multiply** <note>original: *vermultipliciren*</note> itself in **quality and quantity** <note>original: *qualitate & quantitate*</note>. Thus (should it reach the final stage), it would increase infinitely in power and virtue. 

You must **continue** <note>original: *continuiren*</note> with such work and carefully **observe** <note>original: *observiren*</note> everything, just as you **proceeded** <note>original: *procediret*</note> the first and second time, until the seventh rotation has been completed. When this goal has been successfully attained, you will have come into possession of such a treasure that nothing in the world can be compared to it, no matter how great or precious it may be. Thereafter, you will lack neither wealth nor health. For—

<summary>This page details the "Multiplication" phase of the alchemical Great Work, where the red tincture is mixed with Sophic Mercury and repeatedly refined through seven "Rotations" to increase its potency. The process results in the expulsion of "dead earth" and the creation of an incomparable treasure that grants both wealth and health.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the "Multiplication" or "Augmentation" phase of the Great Work, where the alchemist increases the potency and quantity of the newly created Philosopher's Stone before it is used for healing or transformation.</meta>

### Of the Great Tincture

Then you can divide this **Medicine** <note>In alchemy, the "Medicine" refers to the Philosopher’s Stone itself, which was believed to heal both diseased metals and the human body.</note> into two **parts**; with one part, you may continue to **augment** it—**by adding new ferment of the Sun and Sophic Mercury infinitely** <note>original: *addito novo fermento Solis & Mercurii Sophici in infinitum usque*; "Sun" refers to gold, and "Sophic Mercury" to the purified mercury of the philosophers.</note>—while the other part you may **apply** as a **Medicine** and for **Projection** onto the **metals** <term>Projection: the final stage of alchemy where a small amount of the Stone is cast upon molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver</term>. As a **Medicine**, it must not be used in a dose larger than half to a whole **grain** <note>a "grain" was a very small unit of weight, roughly 65 milligrams</note> at one time, because of its great **subtlety** and **penetration**, which it has acquired through the many **rotations** <note>original: *Rotationum*; the repeated cycles of dissolving and solidifying the substance to increase its power</note>.

**In its power of tinging** <note>original: *In Virtute tingendi*; the ability of the substance to "color" or transform the internal nature of a metal</note>, however, it will not prove its **effect** until it has been mixed with gold for so long that it no longer turns the gold into a brittle glass, but transforms the gold into pure **Tincture**. From such a **Tincture**, one must then apply it to the other imperfect **metals**, and it will **tinge** them into the finest gold.

The other part of this **Tincture**, however, you may further **augment** in the following manner, namely: <term>Take</term> <note>The symbol ℞ stands for "Recipe" or "Take."</note> one and a half parts of the same; six parts of a well-prepared **Calx of gold** <term>Calx of gold: gold that has been reduced to a fine powder through heat or chemical action</term> or gold leaf; and two parts of **flowing Sophic Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii Sophici currentis*</note>. Rub everything well together, put it into the **phial**, **seal** it well, and place it in the **Athanor** <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a steady, uniform heat for long periods</term>. Then, provide fire **by degrees** <note>original: *per gradus*; slowly increasing the temperature</note> according to the appearing colors, until it reaches complete **fixation** <note>the state where a substance becomes stable and no longer evaporates or changes when under heat</note>, **proceeding in all things** <note>original: *in omnibus procedendo*</note> as was taught in the first **process of the moist way** <note>original: *Process de via humida*; the "moist way" involves using liquids and slower, more gentle heat compared to the "dry way."</note>. You can **repeat** these **augmentations or fermentations** <note>original: *augmentationes sive fermentationes*</note> as many times as you please; however, not easily more than 5 times, because of the excessively great fire and the **subtlety** of the **Tincture**.

# Short Report /

If you wish to prepare this **Tincture for White** <note>original: *Tinctur auf Weiß*; the alchemical stage aimed at producing silver</note> / there is nothing else to observe than that instead of gold, silver is taken and added as the **ferment** <term>Ferment: a substance added to the alchemical base to "direct" its transformation toward a specific metal</term> / and it is **treated** <note>original: *tractirt*</note> in all things / as happens with the Tincture for Red / which was taught in the preceding section. In the **projection** <term>Projection: the final stage of the Great Work, where the alchemical medicine is cast upon base metals to transform them into gold or silver</term> upon the metals, it must be **observed** <note>original: *observiret*</note> / that the tincture is first mixed with three or four parts of silver / kept in a **luted** <note>original: *verlutirten*; sealed with airtight clay or cement</note> crucible for two days and nights in the fire / then some of such a mass is cast upon the **imperfect metals** <note>original: *imperfecta Metalla*; base metals like lead or tin that are not yet "noble"</note> in flux / so it transforms them into good silver. In this **dry way** <term>Dry Way: an alchemical process using high heat and crucibles, as opposed to the "wet way" involving liquids and glass vessels</term> no **partial works** <note>original: *particularia*; localized or incomplete alchemical transformations</note> are to be made / as in the wet way / which we wish to report herewith to the inclined lover of the art for his information.

If God gives you success and blessing / then use it to the honor of God / and for the advancement of the welfare of your neighbor. Although I also confess / that I never undertook to **work** <note>original: *laboriren*</note> this process / because I hold with those / who seek the **chaotic matter** <note>original: *Materiam Chaoticam*; the primal, unformed substance of the universe</note> & **universal solvent** <note>original: *Menstruum universale*; a liquid capable of dissolving all substances to their essence</note> in the **microcosm** <note>original: *Micro-Cosmo*; literally "the little world," usually referring to the human body as a reflection of the universe</note> ; as indeed the latter shared **writing** <note>original: *Scriptum*</note> clearly shows / and certainly the **preparation** <note>original: *Elaboration*</note> falls far easier and not so dangerously. For now, I wish to add a certain process / which was **shared** <note>original: *communiciret*</note> with a high **Electoral** <note>original: *Churfürstl.*; referring to one of the Prince-Electors of the Holy Roman Empire</note> personage / though without naming the matter / before I proceed to our own matter. And I have wished to add such for the sake of...

## Third Process

### Should / according to the teaching of **Basilius Valentinus** / be arranged in the **wet way** <term>Via humida: the "wet way," an alchemical path using liquid solutions and glass vessels over gentle heat, rather than high-fire crucibles</term>.

Just as all rooms in a castle or a large house can be unlocked and opened by a **master key** / so it is also with the **study** <note>original: *Studio*</note> of the true **Hermetic art** <term>Hermetic Art: alchemy, named after the legendary sage Hermes Trismegistus</term> / namely, that if someone has laid a proper primary foundation in it, and has become certain in its **practice** <note>original: *Praxi*</note> / such a person can afterwards easily understand and explain the writings of the **philosophers**.

It cannot be denied that every **philosopher** had a particular **method of proceeding** <note>original: *procedendi modum*</note> / yet they all come from one **foundation** / and **in the end** <note>original: *in fine*</note> they achieve one and the same purpose; as

<summary>This page introduces the "Third Process" of the Great Work, specifically following the "wet way" associated with the legendary alchemist Basilius Valentinus. The author emphasizes that while different masters used various methods, they all shared a single fundamental truth that acts as a "master key" to the art.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Third Process" according to Basilius Valentinus. It follows the previous page's introduction to the "wet way" of alchemy, moving from general praise of the method to specific chemical principles involving the "liquid key" and the reduction of gold into its primary spiritual components.</meta>

...they set up the work / thus they also received a tincture. If one now carefully **considers** <note>original: *consideriret*</note> Basilius Valentinus in his true meaning / and how he prepared his true medicine / one will find / that he made / and used the same solely from **philosophical gold** <term>Philosophical Gold: not common gold, but the purified essence or "soul" of the metal used in alchemy</term> / and also from bodily gold / after he had **reduced** <note>original: *reduciret*</note> it into its three spiritual **principles** <term>Tria Prima: the three primary substances of alchemy—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—which were thought to compose all matter</term>, namely, **Salt** <note>original: 🜔 (Sal)</note>, **Sulfur** <note>original: 🜍 (Sulphur)</note> & **Mercury** <note>original: ☿ (Mercurius)</note> by his **Spirit of Mercury** <note>original: *Spiritum Mercurii*</note> / then **conjoined** <note>original: *conjungiret*</note> them again / and formed them into a **universal medicine** <term>Medicina universalis: a panacea believed to cure all diseases and prolong life</term> : all of which he accomplished solely through the **liquid key** <note>original: *liquorischen Schlüssel*</note> / which, however, is nothing other / than our Note Well: ***Saturnine coagulated water*** <note>original: *Aqua coagulata Saturnina*; likely a lead-based solvent or "vinegar of the philosophers"</note>, as described in the first process with all its practical steps. Basilius partly kept its preparation secret / and partly indicated and presented it **figuratively** <note>original: *figuratè*</note> with very dark words.

But so that the lover of the art may know / that more can flow and arise from one path / I will faithfully reveal the **method of proceeding** <note>original: *Modum procedendi*</note> of Basilius Valentinus, **with all its circumstances** <note>original: *cum omnibus circumstantiis*</note> / and communicate his true **practice** <note>original: *Praxin*</note>.
