This happens first **through minerals**, namely **through Antimony and Vitriol**, as the first parents of the metals: Father and Mother. Just as children are now nourished by the mother—not that they devour the mother skin and hair, but are **nourished** and **sustained** by her milk and blood (of which milk is the most subtle part, and is **generated** from it)—so also the **metals** are not nourished in such a way that they devour and consume the **whole of the Antimony and Vitriol**, but...

[quasi] prima ad Metallum perfectum*</note>, are cast, so that they may take nourishment from it, and thus grow and pass into **more-than-perfection** <note>original: *plusquam perfectionem*; a state where the metal is not only pure but has the power to transmute other metals</note>. # Short Report /

...heart's desire fulfilled; because Nature has placed all its **powers** and **virtues** <note>original: *vires* and *virtutes*</note> into Vitriol and **Antimony** <note>original: *Antimonium*</note>, as **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> writes in **"Of Natural Things"** <note>original: *De rebus naturalibus*</note>, pages 232 and 259. However, they are not distinguished from one another **essentially** <note>original: *essentialiter*</note>, but rather **accidentally** <note>original: *accidentaliter*; meaning their core nature is the same, but their outward conditions differ</note> / because of the superfluous sulfurous earths clinging to them / which in **Antimony** prevent the equality of the **three principles**—**Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury** / <note>original: "quo minus inter se æqualiter conjungi queant, & consequenter majore vel minore digestione opus habent"</note> so that they cannot be equally joined together, and consequently they have need of a greater or lesser **digestion** <note>the alchemical process of maturation through gentle heat</note>. From this arises the **perfection of Metals**, and likewise the **imperfection** of the remaining metals; which agree with each other along with the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and **Moon** <note>Silver</note> regarding their **form** (namely pure <term>🜍: Sulfur</term>) and their **matter** (namely pure **Mercury** and pure **Salt**). Yet they differ among themselves only accidentally, by reason of the equal or unequal proportion of the **three principles** in the Metals. 

Thus / the **three principles** in all **imperfect Metals** consist of an unequal proportion of weight / because in one there is more or less of the **three principles** than in the other / although these three Principles are present in themselves in all metals / and that is caused by the **impure arsenical water** <note>original: *aqua arsenicalis impura*</note> adhering to imperfect metals, which hinders digestion, so that in this way a shorter or longer digestion—and consequently the **perfection or imperfection** of the Metal—follows, which is not found in the **Sun** and **Moon**. From this also the principles of those imperfect metals are joined **in equal parts** <note>original: *anaticè*; a pharmaceutical term meaning in equal proportions</note>, so that they may be compact and perfect.

Furthermore, one must well know and understand / if one wishes to follow **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, the legendary alchemist and monk</note> in the work / what sort of **Main Pieces** and **Foundation-Pil-**

# Short Report /

...made from a wet, premature **Mercury** <term>Mercury: in alchemy, this refers to the "spirit" or fluid principle of a substance, not just the liquid metal</term> or volatile white **Spirit of Mercury**, and a wet, volatile **Sulfuric Soul**, and a dry **astral Salt**; which Salt must be dissolved in these both, and brought together with them into a water. # Concerning the Great Tincture.

...as found in Basilius [Valentine] in *On the Microcosm* <note>original: *de Microcosmo*</note> page 125. Likewise in *On the Natural Secrets of the 7 Planets* <note>original: *de secretis natural. 7. planetar.*</note> page 211, concerning **Dry Water** <note>original: *Aqua sicca*</note>. Jean de Monte-Snyder, in *Universal Medicine* <note>original: *de Medicin. univers.*</note> page 31, says: "Dry water is not only Mercury, but also Sulfur." For the **Sulfur of Vitriol** <term>Vitriol: a sulfate mineral, often called "green lion" in alchemy, prized for its acidic spirit</term> joined with the **Mercury of Antimony**, from which it is then called a Sulfur participating in a mercurial nature—acting like male seed with female seed—creates a water that is simultaneously wet and dry, according to Jean de Monte-Snyder in *Universal Medicine* page 43. And this is the substance, or the purest mercurial essence: a "dry Sulfur" of its own, containing within its innermost parts the metallic form and the male seed, while participating in the nature of the mercurial female seed. This is elsewhere called, by Agricola and in *The Great and Little Peasant* <note>original: *dem groß und Kleinen Bauer*</note>, the **white and red lily** <note>original: *lilium album & rubrum*</note>.

Without **living and sublimated Mercury** <term>Mercury: here referring to "Philosophical Mercury," a refined substance believed to be the root of all metals</term>, in truth nothing can be accomplished or made in Chemistry, nor can its assistance be dispensed with. For you would never be able to bring the **Martial Regulus of Antimony** <term>Martial Regulus: a purified form of antimony produced by smelting the ore with iron (Mars)</term>—which is the "first mass of the Sages" <note>original: *primam Sophorum molem*</note>—into a solution according to nature, nor elicit from it the right, true Mercury. Furthermore, you would not be able to invert this into a "mercurial water"; for the same must unite with the Regulus—acting as the **Chaos of the Sages** <term>Chaos: the raw, unformed matter at the beginning of the alchemical "Great Work"</term>—through both the dry and wet paths <note>original: *per viam siccam & humidam*</note>, after the proper digestion and putrefaction <note>original: *poſt debitam digeſtionem & putrefactionem*</note> with the Regulus, by means of the wet path. This is testified in the *Metamorphosis of the Planets* <note>original: *Metamorph. Planetarum*</note> pages 33 and 34, and Jean de Monte-Snyder's *Universal Medicine* page 112. Likewise in *On the Repetition of the Universal Stone* <note>original: *de repetit. Lapid. Univerſ.*</note>.

# Of the Great Tincture

Accordingly, **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> desires only to be sharpened by the **Salt of Nature** <note>original: *Sal Naturæ*</note> and its **associate, Antimony** <note>represented by the symbol ♁; alchemists believed the 'sharpness' or acidity of salts was necessary to 'awaken' the metal</note>. Thirdly, if you can mix its soul with **Mercury**, then no secret will be hidden from you; Mercury is **externally** <note>original: *externè*</note> cold. *Metamorphosis of the Planets, page 29.*

Therefore, **Sublimated Mercury** <term>Sublimated Mercury: a chemical state where mercury is vaporized and reformed as a solid; here it refers to a purified philosophical state</term> has also been given special names by the Philosophers, from which it can clearly be seen that without it, nothing can be started in the Philosophical Work; rather, it must always be used alongside it. It is also called **the winged bird**, which is joined together with the **starred earth** <note>this likely refers to the "Star Regulus" of antimony, which forms star-like crystalline patterns upon cooling</note> until they both become volatile. **According to** <note>original: *Juxta*</note> Philalethes' *Marrow of Alchemy, pages 122 and 123*: **The bird bears a star on its tail.** *Metamorphosis of the Planets*: **Mercury became a bird that covered the entire earth with its tail, page 121.**

Likewise, Basil Valentine in *On the Repetition of the Universal Stone*: **Mercury is a volatile or winged bird, which is endowed with swift feathers, page 105.** And in the *Second Key*, see the illustrations on pages 29 and 37: **A mediator between the highest and the lowest, a coupler between the celestial and the terrestrial, water and fire.**

Joined by **an insoluble and inseparable love; likewise a doubled nature** <note>original: *Amore inſolubili & inſeparabili; Item Natura duplicata*</note>, cold and moist, yet hot and dry; related to **Antimony** as our **Saturn**, and the **Vitriol of Venus** <term>Vitriol of Venus: a term for copper sulfate, believed to contain a hidden green fire or "lion"</term>, **which was sublimated with vitriol**, and thus ignited **through the intercourse of Venus** <note>original: *per concubitum Veneris*</note>, the peacemaker. *Metamorphosis of the Planets, 29.* **Living Mercury is a celestial spirit—** <note>original: *Mercurius vivus eſt cœleſtis ſpiri-*</note>

# Brief Report /

...itual fire, living and always burning, which awakens dead metals and gives them life <note>original: "spiritualis ignis, vivus, & semper ardens, qui mortua Metalla exsuscitet, vivificat"</note>. **Quicksilver** <note>original: "Erquick- Silber"</note> is the **Eagle of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Aquila Philosophorum"</note>, the **Philosophical Air** <note>original: "Philosophische Lufft"</note>, and the **Philosophical Salt Armoniac** <note>original: "Sal armoniacum Philosophorum"; a reference to ammonium chloride, which alchemists used to make substances "fly" or become volatile</note>, which is winged. *Metamorphosis of the Planets, pages 29 and 30.* Mercury is a pure fire through and through. Also, **Jean de Monte-Snyder** in *On Universal Medicine, page 39*: He flies away quickly; he resolves himself spiritually into an incombustible oil <note>original: "oleum incombustibile"; a legendary substance that could withstand fire without being consumed</note>.

The **black earthly eagle**, which is exalted through the composition of **Neptune** <note>original: "compositionem Neptuni"; likely referring to a salt or solvent associated with the sea</note> and **Salt of Venereal property** <note>referring to the properties of copper, which was associated with the planet Venus</note> into a crystalline sublimate. By what means or instrument metals are generated and destroyed <note>original: "Quo medio, seu instrumento metalla generantur & destruuntur"</note> must also be known here, so that you may be skilled in arranging your work accordingly; for much depends on this.

This occurs (1.) through the **white sulfur** in Antimony, and (2.) through the **red sulfur** of Vitriol. # On the Great Tincture.

...are broken down <note>original: "struuntur"; likely completing "destruuntur," meaning to dismantle or dissolve the physical form of the metal</note> and resolved into the **First Matter** <term>First Matter: *Prima Materia*, the primordial, undifferentiated substance from which all things are created</term>—that is, into **Mercury** and **Sulphur** (which are contained within them). To these is added a third: namely, the **Spirit of the Salt of Mars** <note>original: "Spiritum Salis Martis"; likely referring to an acidic distillate derived from iron sulfate</note> according to the **Fifth Key of Basil Valentine**, page 42.

Truly, the **White Sulphur** must first be joined with the **Red Sulphur** in the form of a **mercurial water**. This water, like common water, contains within itself a **fiery fire** <term>🜂: The alchemical symbol for Fire</term>, for which reason it is called **Alkahest** <term>Alkahest: A legendary universal solvent capable of dissolving any substance into its primary components</term>, or the **Hellish Fire of the Metals**, through which they were born. Therefore, one must follow Nature and dissolve the metals again with this fire, bringing them to **putrefaction** <note>original: "faulend machen"; the stage of decay or "blackness" (*nigredo*) necessary for the death and rebirth of the matter</note>.

However, everything that is intended to bring other things into putrefaction must itself first have been formed from two opposing things. For example, from **Fire and Water**, which must also happen here in this Basilian process. For common salt cannot overthrow a mountain if it has not first become a burning **Sun** <term>☉: Gold or the Solar principle</term> through the influence of the **Air**. If you now wish to possess the **Mercury of the Philosophers**, create it according to this prescribed rule: Give this greatest of all fires—that is, the **Vitriol** <note>Vitriol refers to various sulfate minerals; here it represents a highly reactive, acidic "fire"</note>—a **cold wife**; let them hold their wedding-bed in **Hell**, and they shall both leave behind **children of wealth and health** <note>This metaphor describes the chemical union of an acidic "masculine" agent and a receptive "feminine" agent to produce the desired alchemical result</note>.

A true **Philosopher** <note>In this context, an alchemist or seeker of natural wisdom</note> must also not be ignorant of what substance the **Metals** possess before they are hardened and coagulated into solid metal, if he intends to understand anything of value in **True Chemistry**. Otherwise, he will find little success in the **Philosophical Labors**...

Now, such a vapor arises **from a smoky breath** <note>original: "ex halitu fumoso"</note>, in German, the **fumes of Antimony** <term>Antimony: A metalloid used to purify gold; in alchemy, it represents the "Grey Wolf" that devours all metals</term>, otherwise known as the **Dragon with Wings** <note>The Winged Dragon symbolizes a volatile substance that can rise or evaporate</note>; (for **Mercury** <term>☿: the symbol for Mercury</term> through the addition of **Sublimated Mercury** resolves itself into a smoky, chaotic, and primordial or cloudy mercurial water). Such oily antimonial mountain-fumes now mix (or bind themselves together, volatile and fixed) with the other **vitriolic** <term>Vitriol: A class of sulfate minerals, usually highly acidic when distilled into "oil"</term> acidic mountain-fumes; for the **Dragon C.** <note>referring to the Mercury of Antimony</note> **does not die except with its brother** <note>original: "non moritur, nisi cum fratre suo"</note>, namely, with the **Oil of Vitriol** <term>🜖le: Oil of Vitriol, or concentrated sulfuric acid</term> itself. Whence **Basil** says in his *Chariot of Antimony*: The **Signet-Star** <term>Signet-Star: The Star Regulus of Antimony, a crystalline star pattern that forms on the surface of the metal during cooling</term> is often brought through the fire with the **Stone-Serpent**, where they have shared the **Sweat-Bath** <note>A process of heating substances together until they vaporize and condense</note> together in the **depths of the furnace** <note>original: "Höllen," literally 'hells,' a term for the intense heat of the chemical furnace</note>; **and thus, both in the dry way and the wet way, like is drawn under the yoke only with its like** <note>original: "par cum pari suo solo sub jugum trahitur"</note>. # Concerning the Great Tincture.

Snyder in *The Metamorphosis of the Planets*, page 121: after the addition of Vitriol <term>Vitriol: A glassy crystalline mineral, usually iron or copper sulfate, prized as a powerful acidic agent in alchemy</term> to the very first mountain-fumes of Antimony, then you would be fortunate. Hence Erbinæus of Brandau says:

> If therefore such a spiritual, volatile, wandering, vaporous, primordial <note>original: "hylealis," from the Greek 'hyle,' referring to the raw, unformed substance of the universe</note> Mercury of the Philosophers, above or below the earth, mixes itself with the red sulphurous Adamite earth <note>Adamite earth: A term for the "red earth" or primal clay from which the first man was made; in alchemy, it often refers to a specific refined mineral state</note>, a body is generated that is watery and yet simultaneously fiery. He says the same in *Concerning the Universal Medicine*, manuscript page 46: visit the interior of the earth, and this Adamite earth, etc.

In Vitriol, or the so-called **red earth**, lies the **unripe gold**; therefore it is also called **Philosophical Gold** <term>aurum Philosophicum</term>. For this reason, the ancients knew how to draw a very secret air-water, a **solvent** <term>Menstruum</term> or **universal solvent** <term>solvens universale</term>, from this salty "dry sea." In doing so, they reported: **Saturn** <note>The god/planet associated with lead and the beginning of the alchemical process</note> drew his secret water from the sea by bright moonlight, from the mixture of both vapors; and then it is called **doubled Mercury** <term>Mercurius duplicatus</term>, made from Antimony and Vitriol.

The **Dragon eats its own tail** <note>The Ouroboros symbol, representing the cycle of purification where the substance dissolves and reconstitutes itself</note> when it binds the volatile and the fixed elements together. Likewise, the **spiritual dragon with wings** embraces the **earthly dragon without wings**; now, by the work of **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire, signifying the heat of the alchemical furnace</note>, there rises a monstrous vapor, or metallic mist-smoke, or a birthing metallic fume; of which **Basil Valentine** <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist and author</note> says in *Concerning Natural and Supernatural Things*, page 256:

> It is a spirit, mist, and smoke, penetrating all metals, and such a mist is the **Matter**

## Short Report

...or Tincture, or the immediate subject <note>original: *subjectum proximum*</note> of the Tincture. This matter, or chaotic mist, resolves itself into an oily, heavy water of a completely hot and fiery quality, thick as blood. For all metals must be turned into a water—specifically a misty, primordial <note>original: *hylealisch*, from the Greek *hyle*, referring to raw, unformed substance</note> and chaotic water—which is then their First Matter <term>prima materia: The primitive, formless base of all substance from which everything is created</term>, and is rightly called such.

For not only the metals, but also plants <note>original: *Vegetabilia*</note> and animals <note>original: *Animalia*</note>, regarding their seed or their first matter <note>original: *respectu seminis, seu primæ eorum materiæ*</note>—indeed, the whole world, according to God's creation and order—was in the beginning water. Specifically, it was a smoking water, which smoke actually arises from the internally-working fire-spirit. Therefore Moses says in Genesis, Chapter 1: "The Spirit of God hovered over the waters," meaning the Fire of Nature <note>original: *Ignis Naturæ*</note> impregnated, warmed, and moved the water so that a separation of Heaven and Earth occurred, as well as their further division into the three principles or kingdoms <term>tria principia: Usually Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, the fundamental components of all matter in the three "kingdoms" of nature (animal, vegetable, mineral)</term>.

Here the craftsman imitates nature <note>original: *Artifex Naturam*</note> and also makes a smoking metallic water, from which all metals further spring, are worked, and ripened; indeed, all metals reach their perfection through this. From this, Basilius Valentinus <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, often cited for his "Twelve Keys" which describe the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> says in his 5th Key: "Our gold has a magnet, which is the First Matter of our Stone," namely, a chaotic misty water, and it is called unformed matter <note>original: *materia informis*</note>, as an undivided essence, or a still-intermingled, shapeless, and divided...

# Concerning the Great Tincture.

...a mass <note>original: *Klumpen*</note> in which the earth is, as it were, waste and void <note>A reference to Genesis 1:2, "the earth was without form, and void"</note>, from which the Heaven and Earth of the Philosophers—namely, copper <note>original: ♀, the symbol for Venus/copper</note> together with the Mercurial Spirit and Salt, acting as the earth—must be separated. Compare here the *Greater Rustic* <note>original: *Rusticus major*</note> page 49; also Basilius, Book 3, *Concerning the Great Universe* <note>original: *de M. U.*, likely *De Magno Universo*</note> pages 254, 256, and 270; also *Concerning the Repetition of the Ancient Stone* <note>original: *de repetit. Lap. Vet.*</note> pages 49 and 97; also *Concerning Things Natural and Supernatural* <note>original: *de Reb. nat. & supern.*</note> page 255; and *Concerning the Office of Metals* <note>original: *de Offic. Met.*</note> pages 220, 222.

It is also highly necessary that the subject <note>namely Vitriol</note> be correctly and thoroughly "opened" <note>unlocked or decomposed into its constituent parts</note> according to Basilius’s instructions, and brought into its three principles <term>tria principia: The fundamental alchemical trio of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</term>; and one must be aware of what is to be observed in this process. Therefore, know first of all that vitriols are composed through the Mercurial Spirit <note>original: *quòd vitriola composita sint per Mercurialem Spiritum*</note> (whence Basilius says they are brought together from white and red). See also Book 3 of *Concerning the Great Universe* page 271; also *Concerning the Repetition of the Universal Stone* <note>original: *de repetit. lap. U.*</note> page 49; and *Concerning the Ancient Stone* page 73; and Book 3 of *Concerning the Great Universe* page 255, acting as a union <note>original: *ceu copula*</note>. Although it may also be made from common Vitriol as if from common Mercury, according to Basilius in his *Concluding Discourses* <note>original: *Schluß-Reden*</note> page 357.

Hence the Philosophers primarily recommend to us the Green Lion <term>Leonem viridem: A symbolic term for a raw mineral acid or solvent, often associated with the Vitriol of Antimony or Iron, used to "devour" metals</term>, among whom our Basilius and Theophrastus Paracelsus are numbered. Secondly, the Vitriols of Mars <note>iron</note> and Venus <note>copper</note> must be resolved again through such a union <note>original: *copulam*</note>, as if through their own magnet. (Whence Basilius: Their improvement lies in the signature-star <term>Signat-Stern: A star-shaped crystalline structure that appears in certain mineral processes, signifying a celestial or "magnetic" influence</term>, or their magnet.) Likewise, from Saturn <note>lead</note> the Philosophers' Stone has received its beginning and first perfection or improvement.

### A Brief Account /

...which Nature must use to etch the bodies. It is the **Dragon**, as I tell you, which burns more violently than hellfire and is compared to common saltpeter <note>original: Nitro vulg[ari]</note>. Because it is cold on the outside and hot on the inside, for this reason, it is compared to common saltpeter <note>original: Nitro communi</note>, according to the same author in *Repetition of the Ancient Stone* <note>original: de Repititione Lap[idis] V[eteris]</note>, page 104 and following. The fire consumes the combustible parts of the superfluous terrestrial and arsenical sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍ris</note>, with which terrestrial Copper <note>original: Venus</note> lived in love, while the water consumes the terrestrial and drossy parts. 

For fire and water are nothing other than two contrary elements, namely: natural fire and preternatural fire. Natural fire is the **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of Vitriol <note>A sulfate mineral used as a starting point for many alchemical acids</note>, like a hermaphroditic fire, which contains within itself both the white and red Vitriol, according to Basilius, Book 3 of *Concerning the Universal Medicine*, page 271. Also in *Repetition of the Ancient Stone*, pages 73 and 94. 

Preternatural fire is the mercurial water made from the Regulus <note>The metallic part of a mineral that settles at the bottom during smelting</note> by means of sublimated Mercury; through these two elements, all metals are resolved and reduced into the first matter. For Antimony arising from Vitriol is called the **Water of Lead** <note>original: Aqua Saturni</note>, and is like Eve arising from Adam; for it possesses a copper-like <note>original: veneream</note> quality and is therefore called the son of Copper <note>original: Venus</note> or Vitriol. The **white Lily** proceeds from **Antimony**, but the **red Lily** <gloss>red</gloss> <note>original: "r." for rubrum</note> from **Vitriol**; the nature and distinction is this: the red Lily is of a dry, Solar nature, hot and fiery; the white Lily is of a cold, moist, Lunar and watery nature, whence these two lilies are compared to fire and water. If, therefore, <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> and <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> ought to reconcile with one another, it is necessary that a mediator be present—or a **Peacemaker** and **Herald**, who makes peace with his staff <note>A reference to the Caduceus, the staff of Mercury/Hermes, which features two entwined snakes representing reconciled opposites</note>—between these two contraries, namely fire and water; and such is **sublimated Mercury**, which contains the four Elements within itself, namely fire, water, earth, and air. # Short Report /

Ertinaeus von Brandau says: learn beforehand what my true **Venus** <note>In alchemy, Venus usually represents Copper, but here it refers to the transformed substance or the "Green Lion" necessary for the work</note> might be.

That a distinction is also to be made between the **Mercury of the Sages** <note>original Latin: *Mercurio Philosophorum*</note> and the **Alkahest Liquor** <note>A hypothetical universal solvent capable of dissolving every substance</note> happens, firstly, in that the Mercury of the Sages is prepared via the **wet path** <note>original: *nassem Wege*</note> from **Hungarian Gold** <note>original Latin: *Sol Hungarico*; often refers to high-quality Hungarian Vitriol/iron sulfate</note>, or rather from **Saturn** <note>Lead</note> and **Mars** <note>Iron</note>, by means of the Alkahest Liquor. Consult on this Basilius [Valentinus] in the book *Of the Secrets of the World* <note>original Latin: *de Secret. Mundi*</note>, page 157, concerning **Sal Ammoniac**. Likewise in *Of the Recapitulation of the Ancient Stone* <note>original Latin: *de Repetitione Lapid. Vet.*</note>, page 104, concerning **Nitre** <note>Saltpeter</note>.

The Mercury of the Sages is prepared via the **dry path** <note>original Latin: *viâ sicca*</note> from running Mercury with the addition of the **Regulus of Mars** <note>A metallic form of antimony produced by reducing it with iron</note>, by the aid of the **Doves of Diana** <note>An alchemical metaphor for the white, shimmering sublimate or the volatile spirit of the metal</note>, which were previously reduced into a **spiritual Solar body** <note>A purified, "light" form of gold</note> through the Alkahest liquor, or even converted into a **spiritual calx** <note>A fine powder produced by roasting or acid treatment</note> through **aqua fortis** <note>Nitric acid</note> and **aqua regia** <note>A mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acid</note>, as Arthephius desires. Secondly, through the Alkahest liquor, the **true Eagle and Root** <note>The volatile spirit and the fundamental source of the substance</note> is prepared, as the **Magnet** and the Mercury of the Sages, from the terrestrial Mercury that has been contaminated and befouled by impurities; in a word, **Venus regenerated** to **Gold and Silver** in both the dry and wet paths.

Thirdly, the Mercury of the Sages preserves, increases, and nourishes the **seed of metals**, and joins itself with them, and grows at the same time with the **Gold and Silver** into a **Solar and Lunar fruit**. Therefore, Gold and Silver are regenerated in their own field, grow, and bear **golden and silver fruits**.

-> ### Concerning the Great Tincture. <-

The **Alkahest Liquor** <note>A hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any body to its first matter</note>, however, does not preserve the seed-like quality of metals, but destroys it, while it resolves and reduces them into their **three principles** <note>In the Paracelsian tradition: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>; moreover, this liquor is prepared from metallic **Mercuries** <note>The volatile, metallic spirits</note>—for example, of **Antimony**, from its **Martial Regulus** <note>A purified form of antimony reduced with iron (Mars)</note>, by means of **Mercury sublimate**, and that with a more vehement fire, prepared according to **Basil [Valentine]** in book 3 of *Concerning Universal Medicine*, page 394.

The **Mercury of the Sages** <note>original Latin: *Mercurius Philosophorum*</note> is also prepared from metallic Mercuries, specifically from **Mars** <note>Iron</note>, or rather from its **Martial Regulus**, with the addition of **Hungarian Gold**, or the **Gold of Silver and Copper** <note>Referring to the "solar" or "perfect" essence extracted from these metals</note>, and this through a slow degree of **digestion** <note>The process of applying constant, gentle heat to a substance over a long period</note>.

Furthermore, it is also necessary to know what the **Alkahest** is in its essence and substance, or what kind of liquor it actually is. First, it is a **metallic nitre** <note>A purified salt or saltpeter, often considered the "spirit of the world"</note> impregnated with the fiery **Mercury** of the nature of **Gold**, like the **Fire of Pontanus** <note>A "secret" alchemical heat that does not burn but transforms, named after the alchemist Johannes Pontanus</note>, whence it is called the **hellish fire**, or the **fire against nature**. Here there are many who wish to fish for and concentrate such **Gold** and **Fire** <note>original: ☉ & △m</note> from the air and solar rays, and to capture it through various minerals of Gold, Mars, etc., while others expose them so that they may be impregnated by the fire of solar and lunar rays; for they say such a **nitre** is for the most universal work, and this is also true. For this is precisely that fire or fire-power that is concentrated and hidden within the metals. When the artist now knows how to separate and liberate the same via the **spagyric art** <note>The alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them in a purified state</note> from its bond, with which nature has bound it under the earth, so that it [returns] again into its...

<header>Short Report.</header>

...can enter into a free spiritual effect, then it works exactly as the former does. And the axiom: **As above, so below** <note>original Latin: *Superius est sicut Inferius*; the foundational law of Hermeticism stating that the macrocosm of the universe is reflected in the microcosm of chemical work</note>, hence some philosophers have attained their **goal** <note>original Latin: *finem*</note> in this way, while others in another way. Yet they must establish a foundation upon which they ground, begin, and intend to complete their work, if they do not wish to **labor in vain** <note>original Latin: *in vanum laboriren*</note>. Most people understand this to be common saltpeter <note>original Latin: *nitrum vulgare*</note>, which however it is not. For our **sulfurous nitre** is best described by **Jean de Monte-Snyder** <note>A 17th-century alchemist famous for his work *Metamorphosis Planetarum*</note> in *Concerning Universal Medicine*, page 109. The reason why the **Nitre** <term>nitre or saltpeter: ⦵</term> of **Antimony** <term>antimony: ♁</term> is not impregnated by **Mercurial Water** <term>mercurial or sea water: ≋</term> is this: because common **Nitre** <term>saltpeter: ⦵</term> and **Antimony** <term>antimony: ♁</term> are only potential universal sulfurs, and in this way they are not generators and coagulators or destroyers of metals, nor actual sulfurs or active ones (**an active sulfur**), such as the actual metallic **Nitre** <term>saltpeter: ⦵</term> of **Hungarian Gold** <term>gold: ☉</term> or the **Gold** <term>gold: ☉</term> of **Silver** <term>silver: ☽</term> and **Copper** <term>copper: ♀</term>. Compare here **Basil Valentine**, *Workshop of the Metals* <note>original Latin: *officin. metallorum*</note>.

These immature **Sulfurs of minerals and metals**, both **white and red** <note>Refers to the *Albedo* (white) and *Rubedo* (red) stages of the Great Work</note>, can be ripened and made useful: first, by the **Antimonies** <term>antimonies/essences: ♁</term> of perfect and mature metals, namely of **Gold** <term>gold: ☉</term> and **Silver** <term>silver: ☽</term>, as those two great, perfect, and ripened Lights <note>In alchemy, the Sun (Gold) and Moon (Silver) are often referred to as the two "Lights" of the firmament of the work</note>, on which see **Basil**, *Workshop of the Metals*, p. 179. Also *Concerning the Repetition of the Philosopher’s Stone* <note>original Latin: *de Repetit. Lapid. Veterum*</note>, page 92. Concerning the combustible **Nitre** <term>saltpeter: ⦵</term> of **Antimony** <term>antimony: ♁</term>. Also in the book *Concerning Universal Medicine*, page 235. Concerning the fixation of the **Copper** <term>copper/venereal essence: ♀</term> of **Antimony** <term>antimony: ♁</term> by the **Nitre** <term>saltpeter: ⦵</term> of **Gold** <term>gold: ☉</term>. If therefore, secondly, you wish to bring your white and red flower to fruit...

<header>Concerning the Great Tincture.</header>

And if you desire to bring it to metallic use (namely, for perfecting **Gold** <term>Gold: ☉</term> and **Silver** <term>Silver: ☽</term>), you must extract the **white and red flower** from the metals through the metallic nature or **metallic nitre**, as the first metal, according to **Basil Valentine** <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, though these writings likely date to the early 17th century</note> in Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, page 249. You must extract this and, with perfect metals, according to the same Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, page 230, perfect the **Tincture** or **Metallic Stone**. Whence Basil says in *The Office of Metals*, page 23: "If you wish to make metal (namely **Gold** <term>Gold: ☉</term> and **Silver** <term>Silver: ☽</term>), [ensure] that from which you wish to proceed is also of a metallic nature." This is the nature which **Saturn** <term>Lead: ♄</term> possesses, as Basil [writes] in Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, who names **Saturn** first on page 48, saying: "This must be done from metals and through metals, that is, those having a metallic nature, such as **Antimony** <note>A semi-metal often used as a starting point in the "Great Work" to purify gold</note>." Likewise **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist</note> says in *The Alchemist's Examination*: "If you wish to make a metallic substance, let a metal be your starting principle. For from a dog, nothing is born but a dog <note>original Latin: *A cane enim non nascitur nisi canis*</note>; nature begets nature, preserves, nourishes, increases, improves, or exalts it." Compare here Basil in *Secrets of the World*, page 166. Also *The Office of Metals*, page 279.

The **solvent** <term>menstruum: a liquid substance used to dissolve or extract the essence of a solid</term> for the **Mercury of the Philosophers** is the **Alkahest** <note>The hypothetical "universal solvent" capable of dissolving every other substance</note>, for through this it is prepared. It is otherwise called the **Royal Bath**, as also the **Bath of the Nymphs**, or the **Bath of Venus**. Primarily, it consists of a natural and preternatural fire. The **preternatural fire**, however, is in our **Saturnine** <note>referring to lead or the "cold" beginning of the work</note> or **metallic nitre**. It is otherwise called the **Clear Fountain** or the **Royal Bath of the Eagle and the Dragon**, according to **Jean de Monte-Snyder** in *Universal Medicine*...

# Short Report.

...can enter into a free spiritual effect, then it works exactly as the other is able. Hence the axiom: **As above, so below** <note>Latin: *Superius est sicut Inferius*; the foundational principle of Hermeticism, suggesting the microcosm mirrors the macrocosm</note>. Therefore, some **Philosophers** <note>Meaning alchemists</note> have attained their **goal** <note>Latin: *finem*</note> in this way, and others in another way. However, they must establish a **Foundation** upon which they intend to ground, begin, and complete their work, if they do not wish to **labor in vain** <note>Latin: *in vanum laboriren*</note>. **Most understand this to be common nitre** <term>Nitre: While commonly known as saltpeter, in alchemy "Our Nitre" usually refers to a specialized mineral salt or the "spirit of the world"</term>**, but it is not. For our sulfurous nitre is best described** by **Jean de Monte Snyder** <note>A prominent 17th-century alchemist and author of *Metamorphosis Planetarum*</note> in *Universal Medicine*, page 109. **However, if the** **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur: 🜍</term> **of** **Antimony** <term>Antimony: ♁</term> **is not impregnated with** **Mercurial Water** <term>Mercurial Water: ☿ial Water</term>**, the reason is this: because common** **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur: 🜍</term> **and** **Antimony** <term>Antimony: ♁</term> **are only potential universal sulfurs, and in this state they are not generators, coagulators, or destroyers of metals, nor are they actual or working sulfurs** (an active sulfur), **such as the actual metallic** **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur: 🜍</term> **of Hungarian** **Gold** <term>Gold: ☉</term> **or the** **Gold** <term>Gold: ☉</term> **of** **Iron** <term>Mars: ♂</term> **and** **Copper** <term>Venus: ♀</term>. Compare here **Basil Valentine** <note>A legendary, though likely pseudonymous, 15th-century alchemical authority</note> in *The Office of Metals*.

These **immature mineral and metallic sulfurs, the white and the red** <note>Refers to the 'Albedo' and 'Rubedo' stages of the Great Work, here existing in an unrefined state</note>, can be ripened and made useful: firstly, **through the** **Sulfurs** <term>Sulfur: 🜍</term> **of perfected and mature metals, namely** **Gold** <term>Gold: ☉</term> **and** **Silver** <term>Silver: ☽</term>, as they are the two great, perfect, ripened Lights. If therefore, secondly, you [wish to bring] your white and red flower to fruit and use**

### Of the Great Tincture.

<margin>F 4</margin>

And if you desire to bring it to metallic use (namely, to perfect **Gold** <note>original: ☉</note> and **Silver** <note>original: ☽</note>), you must extract the **white and red flower** from the metals through the metallic nature or **metallic nitre**—as the **first metal**—according to **Basil [Valentine]** in Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, page 249, and with perfected metals, according to the same Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, page 230, complete the **Tincture** or the **Metallic Stone**; whence Basil says in *The Office of Metals*, page 23: If you wish to make metal (namely **Gold** and **Silver**), let that from which you wish to proceed also be of a metallic nature, such as **Antimony** <term>Antimony: ♁; a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid that alchemists believed was a "primary" metal essential for purifying gold</term> possesses, as Basil [says] in Book 3 of *Universal Medicine*, who names **Antimony** <note>original: ♁</note> first on page 48, saying:

> This must be done from metals and through metals, namely those possessing a metallic nature, such as **Antimony** is.

Likewise **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist known for his theories on the "food of life" in the air</note> in *The Alchemist's Examination* says:

> If you wish to make a metallic [substance], let your metal be the beginning. For from a dog nothing is born but a dog; nature begets nature, preserves, nourishes, increases, improves, or exalts [it].

Compare here Basil, *On the Secrets of the World*, page 166. Also *The Office of Metals*, page 279.

The **solvent** <term>Menstruum: A liquid substance used to dissolve or extract the essence of a solid</term> for the **Mercury of the Philosophers** is the **Alkahest** <term>Alkahest: The hypothetical "universal solvent" capable of dissolving every other substance into its primary matter</term>, for through this it is prepared. It is otherwise called **the Royal Bath**, as also **the Bath of the Nymphs**, or **of Venus**. Primarily, it consists of natural and preternatural fire. **Natural fire** lies hidden in the **little sun** <note>original: ☉lo (solo); likely referring to the sulfurous principle or iron pyrite</note>, or **sulfur-pyrite** <term>Schweffel-Kieß: Common iron pyrites, used here as a source of alchemical sulfur</term>. The **preternatural [fire]**, however, is in our **Saturnine** <note>original: ♄no; referring to Lead or the dark, "Saturnine" phase of the work</note> or **metallic nitre**. It is called otherwise **the Clear Fountain** or **the Royal Bath of the Eagle and the Dragon**, according to **Jean de Monte Snyder** in *Universal Medicine*.

*Universal Medicine*, page 19, and *On the Metamorphosis of the Planets*, page 44.

There is, however, this further difference: that in the **dry path** <term>dry path: via sicca; a method of alchemical transformation using high heat and crucibles rather than liquid solvents</term>, **Salt** and **Vitriol** are omitted, since by them **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is returned to the **wet path** <term>wet path: via humida; a method involving liquid acids, solvents, and slower digestion in glass vessels</term>. Instead, only common running **Mercury** <note>original: ☿ vulg. currens</note> is mixed in without salts by the aid of the **Doves of Diana** <term>Doves of Diana: Columbarum Dianae; a poetic alchemical term for the silvery-white vapor or purified spirit of mercury that "flies" or sublimates</term>, according to the Philosophical axiom: **Nature is corrected only in its own nature—namely, the mature and fixed.** 

Therefore, the **Art** <note>In alchemy, "Art" refers to the skill and operations of the human alchemist assisting natural processes</note> must come to the aid of **Nature** in this study, which happens first: while Art, through the wet or dry path, extracts and produces such or similar matter above the earth as nature forms and the earth generates; by that water, **Gold** <note>original: ☉</note> and **Silver** <note>original: ☽</note> are made. And this matter is called **Mercury** and **Sulfur**; whence **Philaletha** <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist; author of the "Marrow of Alchemy"</note> says in *The Marrow of Alchemy*: 

> Our **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> and our **Sulfur** <note>original: 🜍</note> are only in three metals, which some call Oil and Ointments, etc. 

Note here that the **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> produced by nature within the earth—whether in running form or still hidden in the ore—is not the matter from which nature makes **Gold** <note>original: ☉</note> and **Silver** <note>original: ☽</note> beneath the earth, because that running mercury is already specified and a metal in itself. For it carries with it an abundant and copious **arsenical Sulfur** <note>original: 🜍 arſenicale</note> as the master and coagulator of the purest mercurial water, or **virgin white Mercury**, corporeal and dead <note>original: leiblich und todt</note>—according to *The Open Entrance* and *The King's Closed Palace* <note>Referring to the famous text "Introitus apertus ad occlusum regis palatium" published in 1667</note>; besides being cold and moist. Thus, according to *The Office of Metals* <note>original: Officin. Metall.</note>, the moist and cold alone cannot be a seed at all; it would be...

<header>Short Report /</header>

[The reader] will be able to easily see and recognize [this]; you should follow this in your practice <note>original: *Praxi*</note>, then you will see a happy conclusion to your work, and have cause to rejoice greatly in it. For the sake of completeness, and to provide more detailed instruction, I will briefly describe and present the procedure <note>original: *modum procedendi*</note> in the following, which one can boldly follow. Namely: Take your **mercurial water**, well-prepared and refined <note>original: *rectificirtes*</note> in the previously described process—whichever it may be, whether it was made with the **rays of gold and silver**, or from the *regulus* <note>regulus: a pure metallic heart or mass reduced from an ore</note> of mercury made with antimony <note>original: *regulo Mercurii* ♁tiato (Antimoniato)</note>, or simply from the *regulus* of antimony <note>original: *ex simplici* ♁ii (Antimonii) *regulo*</note>—take as much as you wish, according to your preference. Pour it over a most highly purified <term>oil</term> <note>original: *Oleum*</note> of copper <note>original: ♀ris (*Veneris*)</note> and iron <note>original: ♂tis (*Martis*)</note>, which has been frequently dissolved and recrystallized, and no longer yields or drops any yellow **sludge or earth** in the solution <note>original: *Solution*</note>. Place it for several days in gentle digestion <note>digestion: the process of exposing a substance to steady, gentle heat to mature it</note> so that it may dissolve <note>original: *solvire*</note>. This mercurial water will then dissolve and absorb as much vitriol <note>vitriol: a glassy substance or sulfate, here used as a transformative agent</note> as its nature allows. Then pour it off from its dregs <note>original: *fecibus*</note> and place it again, well-sealed, for several days in gentle digestion; watch to see if it still deposits any yellowish earth or dregs <note>original: *Fecum*</note>, which you must then remove once more. Afterward, seal <note>original: *sigillire*</note> your glass well with a ground-glass stopper (however, three-parts of the glass must be left empty to allow for circulation). Place it in the philosophical furnace for digestion <note>original: *ad digestionem*</note>, and it will, by a continuously gentle degree of fire, [continue]

and afterward change / enter into **putrefaction** <note>the alchemical stage of breakdown or "death," where the matter turns black before it can be "reborn" in a purified state</note> / become quite thick and black / like wagon grease. During the time of **digestion** and **putrefaction**, however, the **Spirit of Mercury** will unite *per minima* <note>original: *per minima*; meaning intimately, down to the smallest possible parts</note> with the inner central pure **sulfur** of the **vitriol** / and thus from these two mountain-vapors <note>referring to the volatile, gaseous nature of the substances</note> become a single one / whose true sign will be / when the blackness vanishes again / and a whiteness lets itself be seen / then the **Body of Gold** <note>original: *Corpus ☉li* (Soli)</note> is completely unlocked and turned inside out in its interior / so that one may partake of the priceless jewel / which is within it / in its highest **purity**. This first **operation** will hardly be completed within 10 or 12 weeks / and before the right **putrefaction** nothing can be begun / rather one must follow Nature here / and wait for the time. For one cannot pluck roses / until they have reached their ripeness and bloom. **This being done** <note>original: *Hôc factô*</note>, then open your glass / place a receiver before it / and distill by degrees **from the sand** <note>original: *ex arenâ*; referring to a sand-bath, which provides indirect, even heat to the vessel</note> your mercurial **liquor**, until dryness / proceed slowly with this. ## A Brief Report /

...[distill] forth the spirit / which in the form of a monstrous vapor will pass over into the receiver. In this, one must take good care / that the fire is not given too strongly / and the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> do not rise too violently / and pass over too forcefully / so that they may cool down nicely in the receiver / **precipitate** <note>original: *præcipitiren*; to settle or condense out of a vapor</note> / and can **resolve** <note>original: *resolviren*; to dissolve or break down</note> into a **liquid** <note>original: *Liquorem*</note>. When the mists have then vanished / their fiery brother follows them / the **Red Dragon** <note>a traditional alchemical symbol for the red fuming vapors or the potent acidic spirit produced during the distillation of vitriol</note> / on their heels / therefore keep steady with the fire / while something passes over / thus the volatile and fixed spirits will have most constantly united with one another. Afterward, let it cool / take out your **distillate** <note>original: *destillatum*</note> / and preserve your received liquid key in the best way. Take the **residue** <note>original: *residuum*</note> out of the **retort** <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> / pour your **liquid** back upon it / **seal it** <note>original: *lutire*; to seal the joints of the glassware with "lute," a specialized clay or paste, to prevent vapors from escaping</note> in the best way / and place it again in gentle **digestion** <note>the process of heating a substance slowly to encourage chemical change or maturation</note> for several days / so that the **salt** <note>original: *Sal*</note>, which still remained behind in it / may completely **resolve** <note>dissolve</note> and transform into a **liquid**. Then draw off such a **solvent** <note>original: *menstruum*; a solvent used to extract essence from a solid</note> by **distilling it back several times** <note>original: *aliquoties cohobando*; "cohobation" is the repeated distillation of a liquid from the same solid base to concentrate its power</note> / thus you will obtain all three **principles** <note>original: *principia*; referring to Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> of the **vitriol** <note>metallic sulfates, often called "green vitriol" (iron sulfate) or "blue vitriol" (copper sulfate)</note> in one. Afterward, **rectify** <note>original: *Rectificire*; to purify a liquid through repeated distillation</note> this also several times **by itself** <note>original: *per se*</note>, so it will leave some **dregs** <note>original: *feces*</note> behind / thus you have then attained the healthy, deep-red blood of metallic nature / which is found to be heavy as lead in weight / and sweet and lovely in taste. With this you can proceed further as you please **universally and particularly** <note>original: *universaliter & particulariter*; alchemical terms meaning the medicine can be used as a "Universal" cure for all bodies or a "Particular" agent for specific metallic transmutations</note>. If you now [wish to produce] the **great stone** <note>original: *lapidem magnum*; the Philosopher’s Stone</note>...

<summary>This page describes the final distillation and purification of vitriol to produce the "Red Dragon" and the "blood of metallic nature." The process involves repeated distillation (cohobation) to unite the three alchemical principles into a potent liquid key.</summary>

...[a]lone to prepare from the three principles / as from the philosophical gold <note>original symbol: ☉; representing the perfected solar principle or purified gold</note> / then you must proceed in the following manner / namely:

can be led and given gradually, according to the nature of your work.

Therefore, place this your work, in the name of God, into sealed vials and cook it until its perfection. No more than 3 or 4 <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical Germanic unit of weight equivalent to roughly half an ounce or 14.6 grams</note> should be put into one <term>vial</term> <note>original: *phiol*; a glass laboratory bottle</note>, for it is an all too delicate and subtle, very fierce and penetrating essence; indeed, it is as it were the **Quintessence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the purest "fifth element" or the spiritual soul of a substance</note> of the subterranean metallic fire, from which the metals took their origin. And this is the right and true essential **first mover of metals** <note>original: *primum mobile metallorum*</note>, which was composed by the **higher stars** <note>original: *astris superioribus*; the celestial bodies believed by alchemists to "seed" minerals into the earth</note> in the first conception in a spiritual manner. This is of such **power** <note>original: *efficacia*</note> that the ignorant person cannot know nor grasp it, which is why it must be treated very cautiously, so that the inner spirit is not **excited** <note>original: *excitiret*</note> by the external heat; otherwise, everything would burst into pieces.

Likewise, three parts of the vials must also be left empty, as already mentioned above, so that the spirits have room to rise and can circulate properly. You must govern and increase the fire according to the progress of your work and the appearance of the colors, and also judge well when the time requires and demands it. During the time of such **digestion** <note>original: *digestion*; a period of steady, gentle heating meant to mature the alchemical substance</note>, all kinds of colors will **present themselves** <note>original: *præsentiren*</note>, as has already been mentioned, but finally they will enter into a **blackness** <note>original: *Schwärtze*; often called the "nigredo" or black stage, symbolizing the death of the old matter before rebirth</note> again, which will last for quite a while and remain constant; thereafter, it will gradually again...

# *Augmentation or Resolution of the Tincture* <note>original: *Augmentatio s. Resolutio Tincturæ*</note>
## I. *Fixation of the Great Stone and its continuation,* <note>original: *Lapidis magni ejusdemque fixatio*</note>

Place three parts of your prepared red, fire-stable <term>Carbuncle</term> <note>original: *Carfunckels*; the finished alchemical Stone, described as a glowing red gem</note> with 1 part of the same *Mercury of the Philosophers* <note>original: *Mercurii Philosophorum*; a specialized alchemical solvent, not common quicksilver</note>, seal the vial well again *in the philosophical manner* <note>original: *more philosophico*; a hermetic seal made by melting the glass neck of the flask</note>, and place it in your *Athanor* <note>a "slow-cooker" furnace designed to maintain a steady heat for long periods</note>, and treat it in everything exactly like the first *fixation*; thus it will again *dissolve*, *putrefy*, and go through all the usual colors, just as happened the first time. You will, however, reach the end of the complete *fixation* more quickly and sooner. For the third, fourth, and fifth time *proceed* in all things as described before, with the addition of new *philosophical Mercury*, and fix it again each time, until reaching complete *more-than-perfection* <note>original: *plusquamperfection*; a state where the Stone's power is concentrated beyond its natural limit</note> (you will reach the end several weeks sooner in each *fixation*); then you have brought your Stone so high in its quality and *quantity* that its *virtues* <note>powers or medicinal properties</note> cannot be sufficiently grasped.

This Stone and so highly exalted *Tincture* does not *tint* <note>original: *tingiret*; the act of transforming a base metal into gold</note> any sooner unless it is mixed with finely filed, completely pure gold (as one part of the Stone or *medicine* to three parts of gold), *luted* <note>sealed with airtight clay</note> in a good crucible, and maintained in a strong *flow* <note>original: *Fluß*; a molten, liquid state</note> for two days and nights; then the gold will become pure *Tincture*, which afterwards must be further mixed with fresh gold until it no longer makes the gold into a brittle *mass*. Then apply 1 *grain* <note>a tiny unit of weight</note> of the same, encased in pure wax, onto other imperfect metal in the

## Comment on this.

The author of this process is very *prolix* <note>original: *prolix*; wordy or long-winded</note> or expansive, and it appears that he did not conceive it *with premeditation* <note>original: *præmeditatè*</note> but rather *impromptu* <note>original: *ex tempore concipiret*</note> and recorded it as it occurred to him from time to time. For it is very *confused* <note>original: *confus*</note> and *obscure* <note>original: *obscur*</note>, poorly stylized, and poorly *connected* <note>original: *connectiret*</note> in several places. It shows, to be sure, that he has diligently and well read the *Philosophers* <note>original: *Philosophos*; the historical alchemical authorities such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber</note>, but whether he always correctly understood, *cited* <note>original: *allegiret*</note>, and *applied* <note>original: *appliciret*</note> them, I will let others judge. *In the meantime* <note>original: *Interim*</note>, a *curious seeker* <note>original: *Curiosus*</note> will still find much of value within it, which will *satisfy* <note>original: *contentiren*</note> him and give cause for other edifying *speculations* <note>original: *speculationibus*</note>. It can also, when *compared* <note>original: *conferiret*</note> with certain other processes, provide and share more light with them, so that they may be better understood and more fully grasped.

...put it into a good and large <term>melting-crucible</term> <note>a ceramic vessel designed to withstand extreme heat</note> / cover it with something / so that no coals can fall into it; then let it be well heated in a <term>wind-furnace</term> <note>a furnace that utilizes a natural draft of air to reach the high temperatures necessary for melting metals</note> / so that the steel glows welding-white. These cycles, poetically termed the "flight of eagles," aim to transform the solid metal into a loose, reactive, and reddish powder.</summary>

# Of the Great Tincture.

leave. NB. The redder this powder is made through the eagles <note>a metaphorical term for the cycles of evaporation or sublimation described on the previous page</note>, the better it is; with that, the second stage of the work is finished. This powder—if it is not sweet enough on its own—must be even better <term>sweetened</term> <note>original: *edulcoriret*; the process of washing away salts or acids until the substance is tasteless</note> with <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>original: *Spiritu Vini*; concentrated alcohol</note>.

Then, put this red powder into a glass vial with a short neck, such that a third or fourth part of it remains empty. Also, the powder must be quite dry and warm when it is put in, before it can draw in any moisture from the air; then the glass must be well sealed by melting the neck. Place it then in an <term>Athanor</term> <note>a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a steady, prolonged heat</note> or in the previously mentioned <term>sand-bath</term> <note>original: *Sand-Capelle*</note> mixed with ash; keep it in constant <term>digestion</term> <note>the process of gentle, continuous heating to mature a substance</note> with a very mild fire. However, the glass must also be well covered at the top, so that the heat can circulate all around it. In this manner, the matter will become black with the first degree of fire; gray with the second; and whitish with the third—though this whiteness is not permanent. Hold it longer in this <term>degree of fire</term> <note>original: *gradu ignis*</note>, and then the beautiful <term>Peacock’s Tail</term> <note>original: *Pfauen-Schwanz*; a famous alchemical stage where the matter displays a shimmering array of iridescent colors</note> will reveal itself with many colors. But when the whiteness appears permanent, then increase or strengthen the fire once more, a little at a time, until at last, with the fourth degree (ever stronger and stronger), the matter is brought through yellow to the highest redness, and has been made <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fix*; meaning the substance is now stable and will no longer evaporate or change when heated</note>. Then take out the glass, break it carefully with a glowing iron, and you will have a stone; test it and see what **God** has given you, and thank Him for it. There now follows:

## Augmentation.
<note>original: *Augmentatio*; the process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical stone</note>

Take three parts of the red powder prepared above, and one part of this red stone; put both into a **vial**, seal it shut, and let it pass—as before, according to the **degrees of fire** <note>original: *gradibus ignis*; the traditional four stages of increasing heat in a furnace</note>—through all the colors, and once again **mature** and **fix** itself into redness; then the stone is even nobler.

NB. This **augmentation** can be continued thus into infinity. Also, he who adds his gold as a **ferment** <note>an "enzyme" or starter substance—usually pure gold or silver—added to the alchemical medicine to "fix" its nature</note> does even better, as the work is thereby ennobled.

## Aqua Regia for this Work.
<note>original: *Aqua Regis*; literally "Royal Water," a mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</note>

Take: Alum, saltpeter, and vitriol, equal parts. Distill from them, in the common manner, a **strong water** <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; nitric acid</note>. In this, dissolve a little fine silver, and let its salty precipitate settle well. Then pour off the clear liquid, and add to one pound of the purified strong water 8 <term>loth</term> <note>a historical Germanic unit of weight, roughly 1/2 ounce or 15 grams</note> of **Sal Ammoniac** <note>original: *Salis Armoniaci*; ammonium chloride</note> which has previously been **sublimated** <note>purified by heating until it turns to vapor and solidifies again</note> over **decrepitated** salt <note>salt that has been heated until it stops crackling to remove all moisture</note>. When the sal ammoniac has dissolved in this strong water, the water is ready and proper for the use mentioned above.

## Note upon this.

This process is almost identical to the first, **in terms of the matter** <note>original: *ratione materiæ*</note>; however, they differ **in the manner of treatment** <note>original: *tractandi modo*</note>, and this one is easier and better to work than that one. It seems more a...

# Fifth Process

## Extract from the manuscript of a *Chymicus* <note>chemist or alchemist</note> from Strasbourg, who flourished in the times of Emperor Rudolph II and prepared the true Universal [Medicine] several times. It is an interpretation of the *Testament* of **Basilius Valentinus** <note>a legendary 15th-century alchemist, though the name was likely a pseudonym for several authors</note>.

HE took **vitriol** <note>iron or copper sulfate, a primary material in alchemical operations</note> and dissolved it in filtered rain water, letting it stand in **digestion** <note>original: *digestione*; the process of gentle, consistent heating</note> day and night; there it deposited many **dregs** <note>original: *feces*; solid waste or sediment</note> and impurities at the bottom. Afterward, he filtered it through **blotting paper** <note>original: *per chartam emporeticam*; standard laboratory filter paper</note>, then evaporated it until only **one third** remained <note>original: *ad tertias*</note>, allowing it to crystallize and dry. Then he dissolved it once more, and proceeded several times in all things as before, until—the dregs and all foreign matters being removed—it reached a certain sweetness and a supreme greenness <note>original: *donec semotis fecibus & alienis omnibus , ad quandam dulcedinem & summam viriditatem pervenit*</note>. He distilled this vitriol through a **retort** <note>a glass vessel with a long, curved neck used for distilling liquids</note> in the usual manner, until the production of a reddish oil <note>original: *usque ad olei rubicundi proventum*</note>. Then he stopped, let everything cool, and in the morning poured the **spirit** <note>the volatile, active essence</note> along with the **phlegm** <note>original: *phlegmate*; the watery, inactive part of a distillate</note> into a flask, and separated all the phlegm as diligently as possible, removing it through distillation <note>original: *& omne phlegma quàm diligentissimè separavit , per destillationem abstrahendo*</note>.

**NB.** The preparation of the vitriol, along with the purification of the spirit, as previously reported...

On top, however, stood a beautiful white spirit; this he subtly poured off from the black [matter], but the black [matter] he further placed in **digestion** <note>original: *digestionem*; the process of keeping a substance at a gentle, constant heat to encourage chemical change</note>. Even more of such a **separation of the spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus-Scheidung*</note> occurred / and the black became like a reddish earth. The spirit he added to the previous [portions] / and the earth / or **residue** <note>original: *remanenz*</note>, he dried under a **muffle** <note>original: *Muffel*; a semi-cylindrical ceramic cover used to protect materials from direct fire in a furnace</note> / where it appeared as a red dust. Upon this [dust] he poured all of the spirit and placed it, well-sealed, in the [steam] bath / where / after a short time / it turned a deep red color. Then he poured off this colored spirit / and drew it off through **distillation** <note>original: *per destillationem*</note> / so that a very beautiful matter / in the form of a red oil / remained behind / as red as a ruby. The separated spirit he [poured] once more further upon...

## **Abbreviation of the Universal Way** <note>original: *Viæ universalis Abbreviatio*</note>

Your Electoral Serenity <note>a formal title for a Prince-Elector of the Holy Roman Empire</note> should take the **Red Matter** <note>original: *Materiam Rubeam*; likely referring to cinnabar or a high-quality mineral ore</note>, which you know well / which should be entirely transparent and pure / and is most beautiful and best obtained / in **Hungary or Istria** <note>regions famous in the early modern period for their rich mineral deposits</note>, which is untainted / by all other darkening metallic **spirits** <note>volatile impurities or sulfurous components</note>, so that they do not destroy the work. <meta>This page continues the "Abbreviation of the Universal Way" for the Prince-Elector. ### Short Report/

Take a lot <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 15-18 grams</note> of the matter, and mix into it one lot of filed **native silver** <note>original: *Berg-Silber*; naturally occurring silver metal</note>. This is silver that grew naturally, or that which has been extracted from **water** <term>▽</term> <note>referring to a solution or liquid state</note> and precipitated, and then thoroughly and diligently washed <note>original: *ausgesüsset*; a process of rinsing out salts or acids</note> with **rainwater** <term>Regen-▽</term>. 

One must rub these together well with the **Red Matter** until they are so thoroughly blended that one cannot be distinguished from the other. Place the mixture into a vial and **seal it tightly** <note>original: *lutirt*; sealed with "lute," a clay-like paste used to make glassware airtight</note> at the top. Set it in the **sand bath** <note>a container of sand heated from below to provide even, controlled temperature</note> for one month, so that only the neck of the tube sticks out. 

For the first eight days, one must provide a **gentle fire** <term>△</term>, such that the hand can easily endure the heat of the sand; this is so that one does not burn its **essential moisture** <note>original: *humidum radicale*; the "radical humidity" or life-force of the substance</note>. During the next eight days, the **fire** <term>△</term> is to be increased by one degree. For the following eight days, increase it by another degree. Finally, for the last period, the sand should be so hot that if one pours or drops a bit of **water** <term>▽</term> onto it, it hisses; however, it must not glow red. 

When this has been done, you will find a white substance at the top of the glass, which is the **First Matter** <note>original: *prima Materia*</note>, or the **Living Mercury of the Metals** <note>original: *☿ Vivus Metallorum*</note>. This is the most secret **water** <term>▽</term> about which all the philosophers have written so much and yet kept so hidden as to how it should be made. For God's sake, Your Electoral Serenity must keep this in the greatest secrecy.

Thus, Your Electoral Serenity now possesses, by the grace of God the Almighty, the preparation of the clear, heavy, and **living water** <term>▽</term>—the **Philosophical Vinegar** <note>original: *Acetum Philosophicum*; a potent alchemical solvent</note> with which all metals may be reduced to their **First Matter** <note>original: *primam Materiam*</note>. This is the **Essential Moisture** <note>original: *Humidum Radicale*</note> or the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurium Philosophorum*</note>: the very beginning of our art and work.

## Concerning the Purification of Our Mercury & the Division of the Pure from the Impure.

When you have thus extracted the Mercury from it, and it comes out beautiful and white, like <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, beautifully bright and glinting like a crystal, then take it quite cleanly out of the vessel, so that indeed no impurity reaches it. # Short Report /

...so that it may be fit to extract the soul from the **body** <note>original: *Corpori*; the material substance</note>: For this Mercury is the spirit which is attractive, and draws to itself the soul of the body, because the Spirit is the Vehicle of the Soul <note>original: *quia Spiritus est Vehiculum Animæ*</note>. Now Your Electoral Highness sees how the elements <note>original: *Elementa*</note> are separated; for first the spirit or <term>Philosophical Gold</term> <note>symbol: ☉ Philosoph.</note> is extracted from the <term>Body of Silver</term> <note>symbol: Corpore ☾</note> in the first preparation <note>original: *in prima Præparatione*</note>, then the soul; finally the body remains dead like ash. # Of the Great Tincture. 111

how one should augment <note>increase the quantity of</note> this Mercury, for we require much of it for the washing of the **Black Earth** <note>original: *Nigræ terræ*; the charred remains or "dead head" left after distillation</note>, as follows <note>original: *ut sequitur*</note>.

Note: Consider here how this Mercury is made; simply take the example of the **augmentation of Mercury** <note>original: *de Augmentatione Mercurii*</note> from sourdough; for if one adds flour to it, it also becomes sourdough, just as it happens in this art.

## Concerning the Augmentation of Our Purified Mercury.

Take 12 **Loth** <note>A traditional German unit of weight, approximately 15-16 grams</note> of this Purified Mercury and 1 Loth of fine silver that has been filed; from this make a small dough, or, as the goldsmiths call it, an **amalgam** rubbed together. Place it into a vial glass, well-closed with the **Seal of Hermes** <term>Sigillum Hermetis</term> <note>A method of sealing a glass vessel by melting and twisting the glass neck to make it airtight</note>, and let it stand in warm ashes until you see that the **body** <note>the solid silver</note> transforms into Mercury, which it devours just as <term>Aqua Regia</term> <note>Latin for "Royal Water," a highly corrosive mixture of acids</note> dissolves **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☾, Luna</note>, and thus everything becomes Mercury; and in this way one can augment it forever, as much as one wishes.

## Concerning Solution, Putrefaction, and Mortification.

Take 1 Loth of our prepared Mercury, well-secured in a vial or flask and placed in warm ashes; then Your **Electoral Highness** will see it rise and descend in such heat, until it ceases to sweat and remains lying at the bottom of the glass

# Now follows the division of this stone.

## The third work.

After this work, Your Electoral Highness <note>The author is addressing a high-ranking noble, likely a Prince-elector of the Holy Roman Empire</note> should divide the stone according to what you wish to use for the white and red <term>Tinctures</term> <note>The "white tincture" transforms base metals into silver; the "red tincture" transforms them into gold</note>. And when you have now prepared the <term>earth</term> <note>original: *terram*; the purified solid matter remaining after previous stages</note>, it becomes capable of receiving the **Ferment**—or the **Soul**. From this comes the saying: what one sows, that shall one also reap. If one sows gold, one reaps gold; if one sows silver, one reaps silver. Thus, if Your Electoral Highness sows into the white, plowed earth, it will bring forth a hundredfold fruit.

### What the Ferment should be.

The **Ferment** (or the seed) that one wishes to place into the earth should be a beautifully prepared **Gold-Calx** <term>Gold-Kalch</term> <note>A "calx" is a powder produced by heating a metal until it oxidizes or breaks down; here it acts as the "seed" for gold</note> for the red stone; and for the white stone, it should be a beautifully prepared **Silver-Calx** <term>Silber-Kalch</term> which is light and white. It shall be put together according to the previously mentioned weight, to be observed as follows:

If the white earth weighs 21 <term>quint</term> <note>original: *qu.*; a "quentchen" or quint is a small unit of weight, roughly 4 grams</note>, then one takes 14 <term>quint</term> of the **Silver Water** <note>original symbols: ☾ ▽; representing Luna/Silver and Water</note> and 10 <term>quint</term> of the calx or ferment. This is to be understood thus: that there be three parts of the white earth, two parts of the Silver Water, and one and a half parts of the ferment.

(<note>original: *aliàs*; meaning in another version or recipe</note> if the earth has 12 <term>quint</term>, then one takes 6 <term>quint</term> of the **Silver-Water Mercury** <note>original symbols: ☾ ▽ ☿; Silver, Water, and Mercury</note> and 6 <term>quint</term> of the calx of **Gold** and **Silver** <note>original symbols: ☉ and ☾</note> or ferment.) These three pieces you shall, with particular diligence, grind very well together upon a stone so that a good mixing occurs. Thus:

Fourthly, I must report even more to Your <term>Electoral Serene Highness</term> regarding our metallic <term>Mercury</term>: When you take this <term>metallic Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium Metallicum*</note>, grind it small along with silver filings, place them together in a clean small flask, and pour upon them a good distilled <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>original: *S. V.* (*Spiritus Vini*); high-proof distilled alcohol used as a solvent.</note> so that it rises a good <term>finger’s breadth</term> <note>original: *zwerg Finger*; literally "dwarf finger," a traditional measurement roughly equal to the width of a finger.</note> above it; then distill it off and pour it back over again, and the more often this is done, the better. And when finally the wine has been drawn over or driven with a strong fire so that the material flows, and when it has cooled, take the residue in its molten state and project it onto <term>Copper</term> <note>original: ♀ (the symbol for Venus/Copper).</note>; the copper will then become entirely beautiful and white, like silver. When that is refined, you will find how much of the <term>Copper</term> has become good silver through this <term>Mercury of ours</term> <note>original: *Mercurium nostrum*</note>, so that one may see with one's own eyes what is gained through this <term>sophistical manipulation</term> <note>original: *Sophistischen Handgriff*; in this context, "sophistical" refers to a chemical shortcut or a "lesser" transmutation rather than the true, "natural" Great Work.</note>, not to mention what would occur if one treated it rightly and followed the path of Nature. Herewith I commend Your Electoral Serene Highness most humbly to the protection of the Divine Almighty.

Gold [☉] / wherein Light and Spirit dwell / is judged only by weight / it allows itself to be divided and joined back together by us / provided that the artist handles it in a right and proper tool / or in a right <term>womb</term> <note>original: *Gebähr-Mutter*. Vitriol refers to sulfate minerals, viewed as a concentrated, acidic power within the water.</note> is to the Earth heart / light / spirit and salt / air and water all at once.
